Leonardo DiCaprio's Catch Me If You Can Is Now Streaming For Free On Tubi

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Katrina joined ScreenRant as a news writer in 2024, covering box office, expert interviews, exclusives, and new releases. Her work has appeared in CBR, All About Jazz, Rising Artists, and numerous other publications. She has an MFA in Creative Writing and has published more than 20 books. She currently writes for ScreenRant’s movie features team.

Known for pushing himself to psychological and physical limits for his work, Leonardo DiCaprio is considered one of the greatest actors of his generation. From blockbusters that blend artistic ambitions with emotional vulnerability to gritty indie films, the actor is celebrated for his range and versatility.

Even before Titanic made him a household name, DiCaprio had already proved his chops as a young actor in films like What's Eating Gilbert Grape, which earned him his first Oscar nomination. After Titanic, he pivoted to taking on more mature roles instead of playing similar heartthrobs and forged a career-defining partnership with Martin Scorsese.

Similar to DiCaprio, Tom Hanks is considered one of the greatest actors of all time, but the Forrest Gump actor took a very different route to building his career. Hanks got his start in community theater and television comedies and transitioned to playing everyman heroes and historical figures.

Instead of pursuing a dramatic pivot, the actor built long-term working relationships with mainstream directors like Steven Spielberg, Robert Zemeckis, and Ron Howard. He also took on commercial projects, such as voicing Sheriff Woody in Disney and Pixar's Toy Story franchise.

Despite having very different careers, their paths once crossed in Spielberg's 2002 biographical crime comedy, Catch Me If You Can, inspired by a true story. Fans can now watch the movie for free in the comfort of their home.

Based on the semi-autobiography of real-life con artist Frank Abagnale Jr., Catch Me If You Can takes some creative liberties and sees DiCaprio as the titular con man trying to escape consequences in a cat-and-mouse chase with Hanks's FBI agent Carl Hanratty.

In addition to two of the biggest names in the movie scene, the crime comedy's star-studded cast also features Christopher Walken as Frank Abagnale, Jennifer Garner as Cheryl Ann, Ellen Pompeo as Marci, and Elizabeth Banks as Lucy.

On November 1, Catch Me If You Can joined Tubi as part of the platform's movie lineup, which allows fans to stream it for free. In addition to Tubi, the 2002 crime comedy is also free with ads on YouTube and included in Paramount+, Peacock, and MGM+ subscriptions.

From Jurassic Park to Indiana Jones and the Last Crusade, Spielberg's most entertaining movies usually combine high-stakes stories with humor and a breathless pace. Frank Abagnale Jr.'s story, on its own, already has the marks of what would make a highly entertaining movie. Spielberg pushed it even further by adding a personal touch.

The Jaws director was particularly invested in Frank Abagnale Jr.'s story, having lived a similar chapter in life following his parents' divorce. He explained it to Back Film in an interview: "I thought it was one of the most amazing stories I'd ever heard... some of this pictures is important to me because it deals with what Frank and I both share, which is broken homes." He once spent a summer dressing up as an executive in on the Universal Studios lot so that he could study filmmaking up close.

It's not a coincidence that the crime comedy landed both DiCaprio and Hanks. Catch Me If You Can's script was so captivating that the Forrest Gump actor pitched himself to play the FBI agent. In an interview with Roger Ebert, Spielberg shared what happened:

Tom had read the script as a writing sample and called me and Walter Parkes, the co-producer, and said “Can I be in this movie? Do you think Steven would let me play the FBI agent? I really know who this guy is.”

In the movie, Hanks plays into the anonymous aspect of his FBI agent and leaves all the spotlight to DiCaprio. The contrast between the two brilliant actors and their on-screen chemistry makes Frank Abagnale Jr.'s story far more captivating.

Prime Video’s Lord of the Rings Series Is Officially Correcting an Early Mistake

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Adapting J.R.R. Tolkien is never going to be an easy job, particularly when you're dealing with a story that spans thousands of years, introduces entire civilizations, and features enough characters to populate several television shows. That's the challenge The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power has faced since its debut, with the Prime Video fantasy epic juggling storylines involving Elves, Dwarves, Númenóreans, Harfoots, and the increasingly troublesome Sauron. While the series has certainly never lacked ambition, keeping all those moving parts together hasn't always been straightforward. And now, one of the people responsible for bringing Middle-earth to television has acknowledged that the show may have been asking a little too much of its audience.

Speaking exclusively with Collider's Maggie Lovitt for an episode of our Behind the Scenes series, The Rings of Power co-showrunner Patrick McKay admitted that the first two seasons occasionally tried to juggle too many storylines, revealing that Season 3 has been deliberately designed to bring the narrative into sharper focus. Rather than continuing to expand the show's enormous collection of characters and subplots, McKay and fellow showrunner J.D. Payne have made Sauron's creation of the One Ring the central event around which everything else revolves. He told Collider:

"You would think that would be one of the real challenges of this show, but actually, the challenge is trying to keep it small. J.D., my co-showrunner, and I have imaginations that we don't know how to stop. They are these waterfalls of ideas, and really we're constantly trying to focus them and make sure we're not asking too much of an audience. I think there were times in prior seasons that, just being our own harshest critics, we realized looking back we were juggling too many balls.

We were asking the audience to juggle too many balls. We're so close to it; it makes perfect sense to us. But one of the things this season we really tried to do is focus it. This is the season where Sauron makes the One Ring. Everything should tie into that. And if it doesn't, even if it delights us, maybe that's not the time for that story."

It's a wild admission to make, but it's one that makes a lot of sense, because the One Ring is, well, the One Ring. And it should be the focus of a show called The Rings of Power. The decision to focus the third season around the One Ring also reflects the show's broader approach to adapting Tolkien's Second Age, which contains major historical events separated by enormous stretches of time.

"We looked at the text, we looked at the history of this particular era of the mythology, and there were these series of events that to us felt like they could lead one right into the next, right into the next, and build momentum with an audience the way that a serialized show should," explained McKay. "And then really the work that we do is taking those fixed points, whether it's the forging of the One Ring — that's a big moment this season — or it's Pharazôn bringing a massive Númenórean army to Middle-earth — that's a big moment this season — and there's others. And then it's about, 'How do we make those feel like earned emotional turns in a story rather than fixed historical events, if that makes sense?'" He went on to say:

"We want to bring audiences to it who maybe aren't anticipating those moments or don't know that they're going to come. If you're a Tolkien superfan and a lover of the books and a scholar of the lore, in theory, there's all kinds of things in every episode that you're responding to and picking out that we're dangling there, whether it's an Easter egg or a huge historical moment, like you're saying. But also, for my mother, who's never really gotten Lord of the Rings, it's not her thing, it should play like a novel does, where it's an emotional experience even if it wasn't in Middle-earth."

The Lord of the Rings: The Rings of Power Season 3 is streaming on Prime Video. You can watch McKay's full episode of Collider Behind the Scenes above.Stay tuned to Collider for all the latest news and updates on the biggest and best movies and shows heading to your favorite streaming platforms soon.

Epic drama set thousands of years before the events of J.R.R. Tolkien's 'The Hobbit' and 'The Lord of the Rings' follows an ensemble cast of characters, both familiar and new, as they confront the long-feared re-emergence of evil to Middle-earth.

Range Media Partners Signs Filmmaker Dexter Fletcher

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Range has signed Dexter Fletcher, the BAFTA-nominated English film director and actor, for representation.

These days, Fletcher is best known for his work behind the camera. This has included being called in to complete Bohemian Rhapsody, the Oscar winner starring Rami Malek as Queen’s Freddie Mercury, as well as directing such films as Rocketman, Eddie the Eagle and Ghosted.

Most recently, Fletcher directed the Apple TV (fka Apple TV+) film Ghosted, starring Chris Evans and Ana de Armas, which marked his debut in directing a large-scale action production and was the most-watched movie debut for Apple at time of release. Prior to that, he exec produced and directed on Paramount+’s The Offer, a panoramic 10-episode series about the making of The Godfather, which earned Critics Choice Awards nominations for Best Limited Series, Best Supporting Actor and Best Supporting Actress. The series served as the debut project from Fletcher’s production company DXD Films. Related Stories News Maurissa Tancharoen & Jed Whedon Sign With Range Media Partners Agencies Pierson Fodé Signs With Range Media Partners

Fletcher’s Elton John biopic Rocketman was a Best Picture nominee at the BAFTA and Golden Globe Awards, won Taron Egerton a Golden Globe for Best Actor and won John and his longtime collaborator, Bernie Taupin, their first joint Academy Award. Fletcher made his directorial debut with 2011’s Wild Bill, which delved into the world of blue-collar criminals, and earned him a nomination for a BAFTA Award for Outstanding Debut and two Writers Guild of Great Britain Awards. Subsequent feature efforts included the romantic musical drama Sunshine on Leith, which was nominated for five Scottish BAFTAs, and the comedy-drama Eddie the Eagle, the whimsical but triumphant true story of offbeat Olympic ski jumper Michael “Eddie” Edwards, starring Egerton and Hugh Jackman. Watch on Deadline

Fletcher began his entertainment career as an actor at the age of six. He played the gangster kid Baby Face in Alan Parker’s 1976 comedy Bugsy Malone before going on to roles in seminal productions such as David Lynch’s Oscar-nominated The Elephant Man and Derek Jarman’s Caravaggio. Additional screen credits include the British TV show Pres Gang and Guy Ritchie’s Lock, Stock and Two Smoking Barrels.

Fletcher continues to be represented by CAA and Sloan, Offer Weber & Dern.

Marvel’s ‘WandaVision’ Trilogy Ended Without Its Original Creator Giving ‘VisionQuest’ a Single Note

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

2026 has been an exciting year for Marvel fans, and it all started with the release of Wonder Man back in January. Marvel has continued this run of success with new Disney+ entries such as second seasons of Daredevil: Born Again and X-Men '97, but the franchise really picked up steam late in July with the launch of Spider-Man: Brand New Day. The movie grossed over $2.5 billion at the box office and is now one of the highest-grossing feature films of all time. Everything in the last few years for Marvel has been leading to the behemoth event film, Avengers: Doomsday, which is slated for release in theaters on December 18, the same day as Dune: Part Three. The final project before Doomsday was VisionQuest, the series starring Paul Bettany that's now streaming in its entirety on Disney+.

VisionQuest is viewed as the third installment in a trilogy that started with WandaVision back in 2021 and continued with Agatha All Along in 2024. Both of the first two shows were written and created by Jac Schaeffer, who has also been tasked with writing the new Stargate series in the works at Prime Video. However, Marvel enlisted Star Trek icon Terry Matalas to serve as the lead writer and showrunner for VisionQuest, a decision that clearly paid off after the show was a hit with both critics and audiences.

Collider's Maggie Lovitt recently sat down for an episode of Collider's Behind the Scenes series with Matalas to speak about the season finale of VisionQuest, and she asked if he had any conversations with Schaeffer about how to tie up the WandaVision trilogy, or if she had any advice to impart on finishing this story. His answer might shock you:

“No, not at all. I called her early on, right when I got the job, because I wanted to just tell her what an honor it was to do this. I love WandaVision. I saw every episode twice when it came out. I would pop it back on. And the first thing she said is, ‘I don’t want to talk about anything creative. You have your blank canvas here.’ And I thought that that was really generous, you know? She certainly could have [said], ‘Be mindful of X, Y, and Z,’ and she didn’t. She wanted us to go do our own thing. And so, that was that. We had exactly that, a blank canvas.”

Throughout the first season of VisionQuest, fans certainly enjoyed the performances from Paul Bettany and James Spader, who was back playing Ultron for the first time since Avengers: Age of Ultron in 2015. However, there were questions from the first episode about whether Elizabeth Olsen would return as Scarlet Witch after she was last seen at the end of Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness with a mountain being dropped on her. It wasn't until the post-credits scene that Doctor Doom (played by Robert Downey Jr) showed up and revived Wanda to use her power. The Scarlet Witch is expected to play a major role in Avengers: Doomsday, which opens in theaters on December 18.

Check out all episodes of VisionQuest on Disney+ and stay tuned to Collider for more MCU updates and coverage. You can watch Matalas' full episode of Collider Behind the Scenes above.

8 Most Perfect Sitcoms of the Last 30 Years, Ranked

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The sitcom genre is rife with some of TV's most unforgettable and entertaining watches, and over the last 30 years, viewers have been well-fed with an incredible variety of comedic masterpieces that have introduced iconic characters, redefined television humor, and delivered countless memorable moments that continue to keep audiences fantastically captivated even decades later. While the list is quite long, only a select few sitcoms have managed to stand out as perfect examples of the genre.

TV series like HBO's Veep, which turns the world of American politics into a nonstop series of embarrassing moments, and the long-standing audience favorite, The Office, which transforms the ordinary environment of a paper company into laugh-out-loud funny workplace comedy, are just two sitcoms that prove that over the last 30 years the genre has delivered brilliantly written and endlessly entertaining gems. Compiled on this list are such gems, sitcoms that effortlessly stand among TV's comedic masterpieces of the last thirty years.

This underrated sitcom is an entertaining combination of youthful energy and heartfelt parenting moments, giving it a special charm that allows the show to remain incredibly enjoyable even decades later. The Black sitcom One on One follows a single father, Flex Washington (Flex Alexander), whose comfortable bachelor life comes to an end when his teenage daughter moves in with him.

One on One is an early 2000s sitcom that captures the complicated but entertaining relationship between father and daughter pretty well. It's a show that is often outshined by bigger hits like Martin, Family Matters, and The Fresh Prince of Bel-Air, but it does deserve its flowers for offering audiences real humor and genuine warmth with a fun, familiar comedic premise. One on One even avoids relying on exaggerated situations just for comedy, instead finding humor in recognizable struggles between fathers and their daughters. It's an underrated gem that definitely stands as a worthy contender among some of the most entertaining sitcoms of the past three decades.

Archer stands as an outstanding animated sitcom of the last 30 years because it effortlessly blends ridiculous workplace comedy with the glamorous world of espionage. The series centers on elite spy Sterling Archer (H. Jon Benjamin), whose competence with fieldcraft and weapons is offset by recklessness, narcissism, and a catastrophic relationship with his mother and boss, Malory Archer (Jessica Walter).

Archer is a pretty underrated series—one worthy of the praise it's received. Its earlier seasons have achieved extraordinary verbal precision, offering viewers brilliantly timed jokes, razor-sharp dialogue, and an almost effortless ability to turn even the most ridiculous situations into pure comedic gold. What makes Archer especially impressive is how seamlessly its absurd humor works alongside its unforgettable characters, their constant bickering, and dysfunctional relationships, making it a great pick for this list of well-crafted sitcoms of the last three decades.

This HBO comedy is one of the most ruthless and verbally inventive shows ever made. Veep focuses on the vice president, Selina Meyer (Julia Louis-Dreyfus), whose desperate attempts to gain influence frequently result in mortifying disasters as she gradually realizes proximity to power can be more humiliating than possessing no power at all.

Veep is an exceptionally well-written series with vicious humor that has been dubbed one of TV's sharpest political comedies. In fact, the show is quite frequently praised as one of the clearest demonstrations of verbal comic engineering. From insults that are not just detachable one-liners but instead cleverly woven into the political rivalries and characters' relationships, to perfectly timed interruptions, rapid-fire exchanges, and laugh-out-loud uncomfortable conversations, Veep transforms almost every interaction into an opportunity for brilliant comedy. The outstanding show, with its clever writing, fantastic attention to comedic timing, and character-driven humor, makes it a perfectly crafted sitcom of the last 30 years.

Derry Girls is an underrated Netflix sitcom that deserves far more attention than it's received since its debut. Set in 1990s Derry during the Troubles, the series follows the self-dramatizing teenager Erin Quinn (Saoirse-Monica Jackson) and her friends, the anxious Clare Devlin (Nicola Coughlan), fearless Michelle Mallon (Jamie-Lee O’Donnell), cousin Orla McCool (Louisa Harland) and Michelle’s English cousin James Maguire (Dylan Llewellyn), as they navigate family expectations, school, friendship, and constant embarrassment.

Derry Girls absolutely refuses to make historical violence the sole subject of its story, yet somehow the series never allows viewers to forget that it's happening. The show is actually an extremely clever series that wields a combination of dark humor, raw writing, and a Northern Irish setting. Derry Girls is a truly rare gem that delivers jokes with confidence and speed—a genuinely overlooked sitcom featuring brilliantly performed comedy and a recognizable cultural setting, making it one of TV's most memorable shows in its genre over the last three decades.

Four Netflix characters, four completely different ways of showing up for somebody. This is the one who fits how you actually do friendship, and what having them in your life would be like.

You do not do friendship at half volume, and neither does he. What you want is somebody who says yes to the plan before you have finished describing the plan.

You are the friend who makes things happen, and so is she. Together you would be mildly exhausting to everyone around you and completely delighted with yourselves.

You have no patience for pleasant lies, and she has no ability to tell them. What looks like coldness from the outside is the most reliable friendship on this list.

You look after whoever is having the worst day. So does he. It is the least strategic way to move through the world and the best possible quality in a friend.

This recently made sitcom is a fantastic reboot of the 1987 hit series, and it has definitely begun to make its mark. The Netflix 2026 sequel, A Different World, centers on the daughter of the beloved characters, Dwayne Wayne (Kadeem Hardison) and Whitley Gilbert (Jasmine Guy), Deborah Wayne (Maleah Joi Moon), as she begins her own journey at Hillman College.

A Different World is an enticing mesh of contemporary student experiences and nostalgic connections. It's already very well-loved as a standout modern sitcom, praised for its lovable cast and relatable scenes. Rather than simply relying on familiar faces, the show instead uses its original legacy as the foundation for meaningful conversations and new comedic situations, offering something unique to this new batch of viewers tuning in. A Different World may be stemming from a truly beloved sitcom of the past, but it still manages to carve out its own place among the best of the best sitcoms.

Tittering on the thin line between sitcom and fun-filled drama, The Good Place brilliantly balances hilarious comedy with thought-provoking questions about life, morality, what it truly means to be a good person, and heartfelt storytelling. The series focuses on Eleanor Shellstrop (Kristen Bell), who dies and wakes up in the presumed "Good Place," only to quickly realize that she has been confused with a morally superior person and must learn from new friends how to be an actually good individual.

The Good Place somehow transforms moral philosophy into useful sitcom plotting rather than simply using it as decoration. With virtue ethics, utilitarianism, and deontology acting as a strong source for character conflict, while also providing truly hilarious situations, surprisingly emotional moments, and meaningful personal growth, the series is impressively entertaining. Of the last three decades, The Good Place defiantly claims a spot among some of the greatest sitcoms ever made.

The Office is a cherished and enduring sitcom whose characters gradually become far more than the subjects of workplace jokes. The series centers around the manager of the Scranton branch of Dunder Mifflin, Michael Scott (Steve Carell), who has a desperate need to be loved, and usually forces his employees into performances, meetings, and "team-building" exercises that often expose his insecurity more than his so-called leadership.

The Office is honestly one of the most beloved sitcoms to ever make it onto screens. Its standing as the “perfect sitcom,” is less about absolute flawlessness throughout its entirety and more about how it totally redefined mainstream, heartfelt comedy. Few American sitcoms have even come close to influencing modern comedy as significantly as The Office. The mockumentary definitely had a hand in establishing the comedic formula that continues to influence TV today, making it a great pick for this list of sitcoms.

This series is completely overlooked as a brilliant sitcom—one so well written that it turns personal grief, self-destructive behavior, and romantic confusion into a cleverly funny and heartachingly emotional experience that feels both deeply personal and hilariously entertaining. Fleabag follows a woman who is only known as Fleabag (Phoebe Waller-Bridge), a sharp, sexually impulsive Londoner, carrying grief that viewers initially only understand in fragments.

Fleabag is one of those rare finds that is capable of completely blowing away its audience with its depth. It is not only an emotional watch, but also a fantastically hilarious one. Almost every well-crafted joke reveals something about the characters, which often tends to catch viewers off guard, as it also hides much deeper emotions. Fleabag may be an incredibly short series, but one that is definitely deserving of tons of praise—an unconventional sitcom that is a masterful combination of honesty, originality, and comedic brilliance, which places it among TV's finest of its generation.

The Minimalist ’90s-Style Coat Will Be Everywhere This Winter

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The return of ’90s fashion is hardly new territory for the Vogue Shopping desk. But if 2026 has proved anything, it’s that our collective obsession with the decade isn’t going anywhere anytime soon. This year, Carolyn Bessette-Kennedy fever swept the headlines for months, coinciding with the release of Ryan Murphy’s hit drama Love Story, which (albeit after a few costume department consultations) sparked a return to the late fashion icon’s off-duty photographs, in search of styling inspiration.

“The online deluge of CBK content isn’t just generating likes; it’s been driving sales, too—a quick browse of the websites of Massimo Dutti or Reformation will give you some idea of just how swiftly brands have moved to capitalize on consumer fascination with this uncomplicated, clean aesthetic,” wrote Vogue’s Emma Spedding in her recent story on minimalism’s continued grip on our autumn wardrobes. She outlined the single-breasted longline coat as a key piece in achieving the sought-after CBK look–alongside a pencil skirt, turtleneck vest and simple leather bag.

Such coats were a cornerstone of the ’90s collections of Calvin Klein, Jil Sander, and Helmut Lang, characterised by (but not bound to) clean lines, understated single-breasted buttoning, discreet masculine lapels and a long, lean silhouette. Usually rendered in a palette of black, charcoal grey, beige, camel, and chocolate brown.

CBK was frequently photographed on the streets of New York in versions crafted from both wool and lighter-weight gabardine, styled off-duty with straight-leg jeans, oval sunglasses and an Alice band by day, or draped over a simple LBD (probably Calvin Klein) with pointed black slingbacks (probably Prada) into the night. Kate Moss, too, favored the style in her early Soho days, often opting for a cashmere camel iteration.

For fall/winter 2026, the coat shape was a key style on the runway. Jil Sander, unsurprisingly, remains something of an authority on the subject, offering a multitude of interpretations, from extra-long and oversized to collarless and polished leather styles. Elsewhere, designers have found ways to disrupt the coat’s pared-back credentials. Dries Van Noten introduced a satin finish and statement pixelated graphic, while Miu Miu did what Miu Miu does best and took the familiar shape, rendered it in crumpled leather and added a fuzzy faux-fur trim.

Unlike more statement-making outerwear that often dominates the discourse, a well-cut, single-breasted coat does a lot with very little, whether layered over tailoring for the office, worn with jeans and loafers at the weekend or thrown over eveningwear as the nights draw in.

Look for a shape that falls below the knee, a relatively clean shoulder line and enough room to accommodate a knit underneath, without swamping you when worn with bare arms. Of course, wool and cashmere styles will earn their keep on colder days, but lighter cotton versions make a useful option for milder weather and transitional dressing.

The long-line ’90s coat makes a convincing case for buying less, but buying well. Scroll on to discover our edit of the best minimalist coats to shop now.

Jim Harbaugh: Broncos took advantage of a loophole the NFL should not allow

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Chargers head coach Jim Harbaugh is not happy with the way the Broncos backed out of a trade for Chargers linebacker Daiyan Henley, or with the NFL rules that allowed them to do so.

After agreeing to trade a fourth-round pick and a fifth-round pick for Henley, the Broncos told Chargers GM Joe Hortiz they couldn’t go through with it because Henley’s physical revealed concerns about his knee. The Broncos were still willing to trade for Henley, but wanted to give up less. Harbaugh believes physical exams should only be to determine whether or not the player is healthy, not to renegotiate trade terms.

“What happened with the trade? Here’s what I know, from talking to Joe: The Broncos based a pass-fail on a medical grade based on whether we would take less compensation, and we wouldn’t and we didn’t,” Harbaugh said. “In my opinion it’s one of those loopholes that the league should really look into, because it sets a bad precedent.”

Asked if it’s permitted for teams to renegotiate trades after the trade deadline, Harbaugh answered, “It shouldn’t be, but apparently it is. That’s why it’s a loophole, and teams should not have the ability to do that.”

Harbaugh said the Broncos offered to say Henley passed his physical, but only if the Chargers would take something less than the fifth-round pick the Broncos had agreed to.

“It’s a medical grade. It’s a pass-fail for a medical grade, and they told us they weren’t going to pass him if it’s a fourth and a fifth, which was agreed upon — they would not pass him for that. They would pass him for a fourth and a fill in the blank. I don’t know what to call that, but that’s what it is,” Harbaugh said.

Harbaugh is right that the NFL has set a bad precedent. By allowing teams to renegotiate trade terms after the deadline, the NFL is incentivizing teams to engage in chicanery on physical exams to squeeze their trade partners into taking less favorable deals. The Chargers refused to budge, but the NFL rules shouldn’t have put the Chargers in a position where they had to make that decision.

It’s a good time to see whether you have what it takes to lead an army through World War II

Steam regularly gives PC players an opportunity to try something new without spending a cent and these promotions are particularly useful for strategy fans, as they offer a chance to test out a new experience before deciding whether it's worth the investment.

One well-known World War II strategy franchise is joining the promotion this week, giving players four days to experience its latest mainline entry for free. Company of Heroes 3 is temporarily free to play on Steam until Nov. 16. And there's an additional incentive for anyone who decides to stick around the game with a 67% discount for a few more days after the free trial.

Developed by Relic Entertainment, Company of Heroes 3 is a real-time strategy game that takes players to the Mediterranean theater of World War II. Released in February 2023, it features two single-player campaigns alongside competitive multiplayer and cooperative modes.

Players can command different military factions, manage resources, construct bases, and lead infantry and armored units into battle. Its two campaigns take place across North Africa and Italy, with the Italian campaign featuring a dynamic map that gives players more freedom to approach the conflict. The game also offers several multiplayer factions, each with its own units and tactical options, making it a good fit for players who enjoy slower, more methodical battles that reward careful planning.

The free weekend also arrives shortly after the release of games's major 3.0 update, which launched Nov. 5 as part of the franchise's 20th anniversary celebrations. The free-to-play promotion runs until Monday, Nov. 16, at 10 a.m. PST. This is a temporary trial rather than a free-to-keep giveaway, so players will need to purchase the game to continue playing once the promotion ends.

Arnold Schwarzenegger Explains to Jimmy Kimmel Why He’s ‘Absolutely’ Still a Republican After the Blue ‘Tsunami’ Midterms

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Arnold Schwarzenegger, action icon and former governor of California, said during his Wednesday appearance on “Jimmy Kimmel Live!” that he’s “absolutely” still an outspoken member of the Republican Party.

“Yeah, I’m a Republican, absolutely,” he said. “A traditional Republican is someone that admires the Republican Party for having freed the slaves.”

Jimmy Kimmel pointed out that Republicans “don’t get credit for that anymore,” and Schwarzenegger replied, “This is the history. This is what I’m about. Someone that has given those slaves American citizenship and given them the rights to vote.” Related Stories Keanu Reeves to Star in Netflix Action Movie 'Maelstrom' After Alan Ritchson Drops Out Alex de la Iglesia on His Upcoming Chilean Shoot ‘The Misunderstanding’: ‘Reality Has Become My Parallel World’

Kimmel then quipped, “This sounds like all the opposite things that the Republican Party stands for now, though, doesn’t it?” Schwarzenegger held his ground, telling the late-night host, “Yeah, but it doesn’t mean I have to change my philosophy.”

Schwarzenegger went on to explain that his brand of Republican politics follows the “Ronald Reagan philosophy.” He pointed out that historically, some Republicans have fought for causes now associated with the Left. Mark Vins’ ‘Reef to Ridge’ premiered at TIFF. Now pocket.watch is on board as its distributor.

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

After building brands alongside some of YouTube’s biggest kid-friendly stars, pocket.watch is increasing its presence in the world of film distribution. The creator media company has come on board as the exclusive worldwide distributor and sales agent for Reef to Ridge, a nature documentary directed by Brave Wilderness Co-Founder and CEO Mark Vins.

Reef to Ridge takes viewers to the Galapagos Islands, where Vins and his crew explore unique habitats, encounter endangered species, and discuss the challenges that hinder conservation efforts around the world. The project premiered at the Toronto International Film Festival (TIFF) where it was part of a showcase curated by YouTube. By highlighting projects from creators like Vins and Ziwe, YouTube showed that its biggest draws were ready for Hollywood close-ups.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

By working with Vins to give Reef to Ridge a streaming premiere, pocket.watch is solving a problem that has stunted progress for some creators with Hollywood ambitions. Major studios are eager to turn YouTube stars into feature directors, but the distributors attached to those projects have often been film industry firms like A24 and Neon. Meanwhile, creators who eschew traditional distribution can encounter some headaches, as Markiplier discovered when he attempted to bring his video game adaptation Iron Lung to YouTube.

As a creator media company, pocket.watch can pursue global distribution for a project like Reef to Ridge while also understanding the needs of a director like Vins. “Creators have built extraordinary relationships with their audiences, and there is a real opportunity to bring that connection to feature films,” said pocket.watch Founder and CEO Chris M. Williams in a statement. “Mark Vins and his team have made a visually striking, cinematic film with the ambition and scale to reach well beyond their established fanbase. We’re bringing our distribution expertise and platform partnerships to that opportunity, with more creator-led film announcements to come.”

As Williams alluded to in his statement, Reef to Ridge is just the start for pocket.watch. The media company is going to help creators realize their feature film ambitions. According to a press release, more announcements of this nature will arrive “by year’s end.”

HBO's 'Big Little Lies' Is Officially Returning, but Its Biggest Mistake Remains

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The debut of Big Little Lies was one of the most important moments in the contemporary history of HBO because of how significantly the miniseries expanded the possibilities of the medium. Not only was it a high-profile, expensive show that featured major A-list stars and Academy Award winners, but it adapted a bestselling novel in order to take advantage of the cultural zeitgeist; this was something that traditionally would have been done with a film only a few years prior, but Big Little Lies had managed to do it on the small screen and become “event television,” despite not being a spectacle-based genre show. Big Little Lies was a phenomenon that hasn’t been replicated, as evidenced by a Season 2 that faced several major issues. Although the notion of the cast reuniting for another outing is hard not to be excited about, it does present some serious questions about what the show’s intentionality has become.

Big Little Lies is based on the novel of the same name by Liane Moriarty, and was adapted by television legend David E. Kelley. The series examines a closely-knit community of wealthy parents in Monterey, California, in which many of the women have put on public-facing personas that mask the challenges that are going on in their domestic setting. The first season is centered around the death of Perry White (Alexander Skarsgård), who had been abusing his wife Celeste (Nicole Kidman) and putting their children in danger. Although Celeste and her friends Madeline Mackenzie (Reese Witherspoon), Jane Chapman (Shailene Woodley), Bonnie Carlson (Zoë Kravitz), and Renata Klein (Laura Dern) are able to get away with their involvement, the trauma of the event causes fissures within each of their personal relationships. Big Little Lies struggled when it went on its own path beyond the source material, and it’s unclear if Season 3 will be able to rectify any of these narrative constraints.

It’s not completely unusual for a miniseries to be expanded into a recurring drama show if the appeal is strong, but adding on to a self-contained story that was wrapped up in a novel can be self-defeating because it is altering an ending that had already been satisfying. Although the ending of Season 1 of Big Little Lies isn’t necessarily rousing because of everything that the characters went through, it did provide some sense of closure by showing that their healing process had begun, even if it never could be truly completed. The issue with Season 2 is that committing to showing this gradual recovery from painful circumstances is not as inherently interesting on a dramatic level, and it led to a season in which the new issues many of the characters dealt with felt very soapy. Seeing Celeste and Jane cope with their collective trauma at the hands of Perry, and how it affected both of their children, was undeniably powerful, but Renata’s financial issues and Madeline’s familial conflict weren’t nearly as compelling.

Season 2 also suffered from a behind-the-scenes struggle, as the show was not able to let a new creative voice take the story in a new direction, resulting in a season that was at odds with itself. The brilliant British filmmaker Andrea Arnold had been hired to showrun Season 2, but her vision was significantly altered and resulted in creative differences that made it a shadow of what she had intended; it certainly was not a good look for HBO to do so much meddling in the vision of a female artist for a show that had such strong feminist themes. While the logical choice would have been to bring back director Jean-Marc Vallée, who handled Season 1 with the utmost respect and grace, his tragic death in 2021 meant that HBO is forced to find another showrunner who has yet to be announced. Given what Arnold went through in Season 2, it’s hard to imagine that a lot of top-line talent would be willing to immediately sign up.

Season 3 is reportedly set to be based on Big Little Truths, a sequel to the original novel that Moriarty published this year. The issue is that the sequel novel includes events that contradict those of Season 2 of Big Little Lies, meaning that adaptive choices will need to be fairly significant if the third season wants to follow the book. Although having an outline does give Season 3 more of a template than its predecessor, it is not automatically a guarantee of quality; subsequent adaptations of other Moriarty novels, such as Nine Perfect Strangers and Apples Never Fall, were not nearly as well-received, suggesting that the first season of Big Little Lies was an anomaly in which everything seemed to go just right. It was a big deal initially to see so many major actresses on television at once, but it is less exciting in an era in which star-studded television has become more normalized.

The more critical question that Big Little Lies faces in its future is that it is not clear what the season is trying to say. Although the first season was certainly entertaining and had some conventional television moments, it felt like an appropriate reaction to a hostile cultural moment for women that examined the complications of justice. The season’s goal was to find a way to set these characters on a better path, and continuing to focus on their misfortune and misery sends mixed messages. Big Little Lies had the chance to be a perfect miniseries, but every subsequent season puts it at risk of undermining its own legacy.

Michael John Roberts Dies: ‘TripTank’, ‘BoJack Horseman’ Animator, Director & Producer Was 48

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Michael John Roberts, an animator and director of such series as Comedy Central’s TripTank and Netflix’s BoJack Horseman, died of glioblastoma Wednesday, November 11, in Los Angeles. He was 48.

His death was announced by his family, including his wife, the One Life to Live and Gossip Girl actor Bree Williamson. The announcement states that Roberts died peacefully at home surrounded by his family.

“With heavy hearts, we share the passing of Michael John Roberts,” the announcement reads. “Michael saw the world a little differently. A gifted artist with an extraordinary talent, he could find something worth exploring in what others might overlook. His creativity was not limited to what he put on paper; it shaped the way he thought, questioned, and understood the world around him.”

Born December 20, 1977, in Toronto, where he was raised, Roberts got his start as an animator in the late 1990s and early 2000s on such children’s series as the animated Angela Anaconda and the animation-live action hybrid The Hoobs, both produced by Canada’s Decode Entertainment (The Hoobs was co-created and co-produced by The Jim Henson Company). Watch on Deadline

Although starting in children’s television, Roberts would become best known for his work on animated comedy geared to adults. He co-created and directed Comedy Central’s 2014-2016 sketch comedy series TripTank, which featured voice work from Bob Odenkirk, Larry David, Niecy Nash, Zach Galifianakis and others, and he animated the 2015 opening sequence of BoJack Horseman (he would direct two episodes of the Netflix series).

He subsequently directed episodes of, among others, the Bill Burr series F Is For Family, the TBS series Final Space, MrBeast Lab, the 2023 Apple TV series Strange Planet and, most recently, a 2026 episode of Masters of the Universe: Tales From Eternia. As a producer, his credits included the 2018 John Cena-Kat Dennings YouTube Red series Dallas & Robo, the 2024 series Hot Wheels Let’s Race and a 2026 TV special from the Hot Wheels franchise.

In addition to his wife, who played the popular character Jessica Buchanan on One Life to Live from 2002 to 2012, Roberts is survived by son McGreggor; sister Jane Roberts; niece Gwen; nephew Vaughan; and cousin Hrothgar Cashmore, whom he saw as a brother.

“We were supposed to have more time,” his family wrote. “More conversations over a good cup of coffee. More spirited debates about matters both profound and wonderfully insignificant. More laughter, more stories, more music and more ordinary moments that would someday become extraordinary memories.

“Although his illness came suddenly and the time that followed was heartbreakingly short, it cannot define the man he was. His life is better remembered through what he created, the conversations he inspired, the friendships he nurtured, and the love he gave so freely to those closest to him.”

Chris Hemsworth and Elsa Pataky are handling a scary situation at home.

The Avengers star and the Fast & Furious actress' Australia home was targeted by armed robbers who stole three of their vehicles, which have since been recovered, police said.

The incident occurred around 2 a.m., when Pataky was sleeping at their home near Byron Bay and woke to the sound of their dogs barking, according to the news service.

The model reportedly went back to sleep, but discovered in the morning that the family's BYD electric vehicle, Ram truck and Ford Ranger Raptor Ute—all of which were parked with the keys inside—were missing from the garage.

Upon checking security footage, investigators learned that a group of men wearing balaclava head coverings—with two holding knives—gain access to the garage by scaling a fence, according to the news outlet.

Police were able to locate two of the cars hours away from the couple’s beachside residence the same day, per 7News. The third vehicle was found on Nov. 12, according to TMZ.

Earlier this year, Hemsworth—dad to daughter India, 14, as well as twins Sasha and Tristan, 12, with Pataky—detailed his family's 2015 move from Los Angeles back to his home country of Australia, calling it "the greatest decision."

Hemsworth added that the relocation was largely inspired by his own upbringing in rural Australia with brothers Liam Hemsworth and Luke Hemsworth.

He was sentenced to six months in jail for Attempted Burglary in the Second Degree and Criminal Contempt in the First Degree.

The Hills alum fell victim to the Bling Ring robberies in 2009.

Having just gotten back from Australia, she left for a party without unpacking. However, when she returned, “I was like, ‘Am I losing my mind? My suitcase is gone, my laptop. Everything is off my bed. What is going on?'" she told Rachel Bilson on her podcast in February 2024. "Then I went in my closet and I called my sister and I was like, ‘OK, if I open my jewelry box and stuff is gone, someone is in my house or stole stuff. ‘I opened it, and everything is gone.”

The Bling Ring, which consisted of members Alexis Haines, Rachel Lee, Nick Prugo, Courtney Ames, Diana Tamayo, Johnny Ajar and Roy Lopez, were all arrested in 2009.

Many of the members went on to serve time in prison or probation and community service. Lee, the alleged ringleader, served a year and a half in state prison out of her four year sentence. Prugo served a year in prison, while Haines served 30 days in prison. Ames was sentenced to three years of probation and two months of community service. Tamayo was sentenced to probation and two months of community service. Similarly, Lopez Jr. was sentenced to three years of supervised probation. Ajar was sentenced to three years in prison, but ended up serving one.

The Lizzie McGuire alum was in Canada on vacation with son Luca when her Los Angeles home was broken into in 2017.

“This is a scary and upsetting situation for anyone to go through,” Duff’s rep told E! News at the time, “but Hilary is thankful that her family, her staff, her home and her pets are all safe.”

While the actress was in Toronto filming Carrie in 2012, her New York City apartment was burglarized and 10 pieces of jewelry totaling  at least $127,000 were stolen, NYPD Deputy Commissioner Paul Browne confirmed to E! News.

The singer defended herself when burglars entered her northern London home in 2016. “Don’t f--king come up these stairs,” Ora can be heard saying in a recording of her call to police. “I will f--k you up, you f--king hear me!” Still, the suspect stole nearly $295,000 worth of jewelry, clothes, iPhones and Mac computers.

While the “Super Bass” rapper was out of town in 2017, her Beverly Hills home was robbed and around $175,000 worth of jewelry and other items were stolen.

The “Obsessed” singer was the victim of a burglary in 2017, when $50,000 worth of handbags and sunglasses were taken from her Los Angeles home. The suspect, according to the LAPD, entered through a window.

‘Russell Brand: Open Secret’ Documentary Set at Netflix

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

From the producers of 'Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story,' the film promises to examine Brand's journey from superstar to convicted rapist.

A Russell Brand documentary is coming to Netflix, set to chart his comedy stardom to a criminal trial that culminated in a 10-year prison sentence for rape and sexual assault this week.

Russell Brand: Open Secret is from the producers of Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story, 72 Films, a Fremantle company, and directed by Olivia Isaacs.

The doc promises to feature inside voices and archive footage, providing insight into Brand’s life and the investigation that started it all at The Times and The Sunday Times, through their chief news correspondent, Rosamund Urwin and investigations correspondent, Charlotte Wace. Crucially, Russell Brand: Open Secret will examine how, despite many people in the TV industry being aware of his behavior, he evaded punishment for so long. Related Stories Movies China's Film Industry Arrives at AFM on a Domestic High Movies Can Constantin Survive Its Own Parent Company?

Brand’s four-week trial at Southwark Crown Court captivated the country and British media. After being sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison on Monday, the disgraced star was led away and transferred to HMP Wandsworth prison. He’ll be expected to serve around seven years of that prison sentence before being released on licence.

The judge, Justice Bennathan, took Brand through all of his crimes in extensive detail, citing the witness impact statements from the four victims before handing down the sentence. He said Brand operated with “staggering arrogance” during the attacks and that his entourage often “indulged” his “dreadful behavior.” He said toward the end of his statement: “Whatever women wear, wherever they go, indeed whatever they drink, they retain the right to say ‘no’ and to demand justice from criminal courts when sexual predators like you, Russell Brand, act without their consent.”

Among the crimes he was convicted of were a violent anal rape of a woman at his Hampstead home in 2009 and three sexual assaults.

Israeli strike on Gaza hospital kills six Palestinians, health ministry says

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

First responders from the Civil Defence inspect the damage at Nasser hospital in Khan Younis

At least six people have been killed and a large number injured in an Israeli air strike on Nasser hospital, the biggest hospital in the southern Gaza Strip, the Hamas-run health ministry says.

Eyewitnesses told the BBC that a single missile fired by a warplane hit a building containing the surgical wards and intensive care units overnight.

The health ministry said medical staff, patients and companions were killed. It condemned the attack as a "flagrant violation of international laws and conventions".

The Israeli military said it struck a senior Hamas figure and other operatives from the group's armed wing who had been operating within the hospital.

It alleged that they had violated international law by using the hospital as "a command centre for military and terrorist activities".

Hamas did not comment on the claim. But it accused Israel of killing patients and medical staff and perpetrating "a full-fledged war crime".

Under international law, hospitals have a protected status, but they have been repeatedly targeted during the Gaza war.

Israel accuses Hamas, which retains security control of parts of Gaza not occupied by Israeli troops, of cynically exploiting civilian infrastructure. It says the group stores weapons and runs command and control rooms in hospitals, which Hamas denies.

Israel does not allow international news organisations, including the BBC, into Gaza, making it difficult to verify what is happening in the territory.

In a video posted on social media, a paramedic from the Civil Defence agency who arrived at the building after the strike early on Thursday said the missile penetrated the five-storey structure and hit a room in the surgical department.

A spokesman for the Civil Defence, which operates under the Hamas-run interior ministry, initially said that five people had been killed and 20 others injured.

Videos shared by activists showed thick smoke inside the building and extensive destruction on the second floor, where medical equipment and beds had been scattered by the blast.

Local activists have also circulated claims that the strike may have targeted a senior Hamas security figure who was hiding inside the building.

Speaking to reporters in the aftermath of the strike, the director of Nasser hospital said the building had been hit in a vertical strike, causing extensive damage.

Nasser hospital was hit by a double Israeli strike in August 2025, which killed at least 20 people, including journalists and health workers.

Israeli strikes on Gaza have continued despite a ceasefire agreement in October last year.

More than 1,620 people have been killed since then, according to the territory's health ministry, whose figures are considered reliable by the UN.

Israel and Hamas have accused each other of violating the ceasefire.

The humanitarian crisis in Gaza has recently worsened as a result of heavy rains that reportedly flooded tens of thousands of tents housing displaced families.

The Gaza war was triggered by the Hamas-led attack on southern Israel on 7 October 2023, when about 1,200 people were killed and 251 others were taken hostage.

Since then, more than 74,650 people have been killed in Gaza, the health ministry says.

Golshifteh Farahani Is a Widow Fighting the Exploitation of Kids as the Taliban Nears Kabul in ‘When She Hears the Bells’ (Exclusive Clip and Chat)

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Chabname Zariab’s Tallinn-debuting debut feature includes images shot in Afghanistan and shines a light on women and a "taboo" topic in a story that is "a personal responsibility and an act of quiet resistance."

Golshifteh Farahani‘s (Alpha, Pirates of the Caribbean: Dead Men Tell No Tales, The Pear Tree) new film, writer-director Chabname Zariab’s debut feature When She Hears the Bells (Les Clochettes de Kaboul), is both a thriller and a call to stand up for human rights.

France-based Iranian actress Farahani stars as Leila, a young widow seeking a fresh start in Kabul, the capital of Afghanistan. But she soon finds out that the kitchen, in which she finds work, is a so-called bacha bazi house. These are establishments in which boys and young men are forced to dress in women’s clothes and dance for entertainment – and for sexual and other exploitation. At the same time – it is 2021 – the Taliban is moving closer and closer to Kabul. Leila tries to flee and also help a group of boys escape before the city falls. Sayed Hashimi (13 Days, 13 Nights) co-stars in the film, which includes images actually shot in Afghanistan. Related Stories Movies Compassion and Bureaucracy Clash in 'A Job Well Done,' Which Explores Immigration Through Social Workers' Eyes Movies "I Look Good With a Weapon in My Hand": Priyanka Chopra Jonas on Trading Weapons for Words in 'Bluefly'

When She Hears the Bells premieres in the First Feature Competition of the 30th edition of the Tallinn Black Nights Film Festival (PÖFF) in Estonia.

Pierre Cottereau handled cinematography, while the editors are Xavier Sirven, Héloïse Pelloquet and Julie Duclaux. Zariab directed When She Hears the Bells, which she co-wrote with her sister Parande Zariab. Produced by France’s Alta Rocca Films with Germany’s Starhaus Filmproduktion, Belgium’s Versus Production and Arte France Cinema, the film will be distributed in France by Le Pacte, in Germany by Grandfilm and O’Brother in Belgium. Le Pacte is handling sales.

Writer-director Zariab, an Afghan creative based in Paris, wanted to take on repression across society. About bacha bazi, she says: “It is a symbolic reflection of the state of this society, where laws enforce the separation of men and women and leave room for certain abuses, of which this is one of the most appalling.”

And she adds: “The story also highlights the reality for women both before the Taliban’s return and since August 2021, when millions saw their rights erased and the world largely looked away. For me, telling this story is both a personal responsibility and an act of quiet resistance.”

THR can now exclusively premiere a clip from When She Hears the Bells before sharing an email interview with Zariab about the film and how it mixes genre elements with a cinematic tribute to solidarity and the bonds forged when standing shoulder-to-shoulder to protect human rights and the vulnerable. Watch the exclusive clip right here.

And here is what the writer-director says about When She Hears the Bells.

Where did you shoot most of the film? And I heard you also shot parts in Afghanistan. What can you share about that?We had first considered shooting in Iraqi Kurdistan, whose landscapes and faces strongly resemble Afghanistan’s. We went on location [with] scouts there, then changed course at the last minute because of the bombings between Iran and Israel in 2024. The film was ultimately shot almost entirely in Casablanca, Morocco.Some of the images were indeed shot in Kabul, because I wanted authentic footage of that city. It was essential. But it was out of the question for us to go there. So I called a friend who is a cinematographer in Afghanistan — I’m not giving his name, for his safety — and we worked remotely. I would send him screenshots of my film so that the wide shots would blend well with the images I had shot in Morocco, or I would ask him to go to neighborhoods and roads that I know. These images are mainly wide shots, meant to capture landscapes and neighborhoods, so that the film rings true without exposing the people who live there.

Why did you want to tell a feature film story about bacha bazi in Afghanistan and what does the practice of bacha bazi say about life and society there?I am very sensitive to the cause of children and animals, whom I consider to be the most vulnerable beings in the world. It must be said that bacha bazi is not the subject of the film but the arena in which we follow the journey of an Afghan woman, Leila, played by Golshifteh Farahani, who tries to save children before everything collapses in the Kabul of 2021.Coming back to bacha bazi, it is a practice that has existed in the region for centuries. It has disappeared completely in some countries. It was also on its way to disappearing in Afghanistan. When I was a child, in the 1980s, the practice existed in remote areas but no longer in the big cities. Then came the Taliban’s arrival in 1996 — the moment when my family and I left the country where I was born to settle in France — and it was completely banned, according to many reports, such as one by the NGO The New Humanitarian.

But it surged again with the arrival of American troops, who had been ordered to turn a blind eye to bacha bazi in order to win over Afghan warlords. This scandal was confirmed years later by journalistic investigations, as well as by official Pentagon reports in 2017. Little by little, having “a bacha” — literally, having “a boy” — became a social marker, a sign of wealth and power. Today, according to the Afghan press, such as Amu TV, “the new Taliban” have “adopted” the practice. According to its journalists, the perpetrators are rarely prosecuted.Above all, it reveals the impunity that persists thanks to the influence of those who practice it. Bacha bazi is only one symptom among others of a violent society that separates women from the social landscape, and therefore from desires. Imagine a society in which men and women live separately and are not allowed to cross paths. To me, that is quite frightening.

You made a short film about bacha bazi that was focused on the boys. I love that your feature film also shows us women’s lives and difficulties. How important was it for you to show us this women’s perspective, both before and after the Taliban’s return, and what do you hope audiences will take away from the film?My short film focused on the boys because my concern at the time was what children endure, in my country of origin as elsewhere. A child who grows up in fear or violence carries the marks for life, and it is through childhood that a nation’s destiny is shaped.When the Taliban took Kabul in 2021, I was developing another project, set in Paris but still tied to Afghanistan. I decided to abandon it in order to make a film set in the country. I could not ignore the cause of women, who already held a fragile place in that society and whom the Taliban are trying to erase more and more. Today, women are no longer allowed to work and girls are no longer admitted to school beyond the age of 12. What I hope audiences take away is the distress of an entire society. I believe my country is going through a very deep collective suffering. It was a deliberate choice to show the period before the fall of Kabul from a woman’s point of view, because the period after, the whole world has seen on the news, and still sees today.I also wanted the film to end on that collapse, and on the question of those who leave, because fleeing horror when everything is falling apart is both a positive escape for Leila, the character, and no guarantee of a peaceful life afterward. I’m thinking of Dheepan by Jacques Audiard, which begins on the other side of the fence.

How did you get Golshifteh Farahani to star in the film? She brings so many layers and dimensions to her role.The hardest part was making contact with Golshifteh. It took several months. The first time I saw her was in About Elly by Asghar Farhadi. I held on to her image for a long time. Then I followed her work in Chicken With Plums by Marjane Satrapi and later in The Patience Stone (Syngué Sabour) by Atiq Rahimi, where she played an Afghan woman and spoke Afghan Persian almost perfectly. That is also one of the films that made me feel this character could only be for her.

Her commitment to women’s causes mattered just as much, because it echoed what I wanted to tell. So I waited until she agreed to meet me. Once she learned about the subject, she accepted very quickly. She is an extraordinary actress and an extraordinary woman. She was incredibly supportive on set. Many of my actors were non-professionals or came from the theater. Golshifteh not only mastered her own performance but also guided the other actors, and almost choreographed her movements with the children to help them act and to keep takes from being interrupted.

She is a woman who breaks down barriers, and we were on the same page about that. In the evenings, we sometimes gathered in one another’s apartments to play music and sing. Golshifteh can play many instruments. One of the actresses was also a singer, and we had other musicians in the group. Those evenings were incredible, full of emotion. We were Afghans and Iranians together, united by what we had in common: our culture and our exile. And Golshifteh was also key in fostering that cohesion. It was beautiful!

How did you decide on the title for the film ‘When She Hears the Bells,’ or ‘The Bells of Kabul’ in France?My short film was called Au bruit des clochettes (At the Sound of the Little Bells), and its English version was When You Hear the Bells. The feature film also tells the story of the country. It is a story within history. For the French version, I wanted to put Kabul in the title and keep “the little bells,” which are somewhat enigmatic — for a Westerner, you don’t know what the little bells are. People from the East — Iranians, Afghans, and so on — know exactly what it refers to.

It was interesting, in fact, because during casting, some of the people we contacted refused to audition simply upon learning the title. They knew exactly what it was about: the little bells are bacha bazi. And they refused because the subject is too taboo, too sensitive. For the English version, since Leila ends up in a bacha bazi house, When She Hears the Bells seemed right to me because it puts the female character back at the center. It is her own story, the story of an ordinary woman who becomes a heroine and whose journey becomes extraordinary.

Is there anything you would like to mention?Thank you. This film was born out of a concern. If audiences come away with a better understanding of what Afghan women, and Afghans more broadly, are living through today, and with the feeling that this suffering deserves to be faced head-on, then it will have done its part. Because indeed, today, the world has once again abandoned this country to its fate. There are so many misfortunes and new conflicts that have emerged that people no longer talk about this population. It was important to me to make this film in order to shine a light on them.

For the financing, we received the trust of many partners, who were moved by the subject and convinced of the necessity of this story. Now I hope the film will find an echo and a distribution commensurate with the importance I attach to it.

Right now, winter is beginning, and the country’s economic situation is catastrophic. People struggle to feed themselves and to heat their homes. Children are dying of malnutrition in some regions. There is no work, no real future. There is also a cultural genocide: attempts are being made to erase the Persian language and other languages in favor of Pashto, the language spoken by most of the Taliban.

Very recently I read that in a Hazara neighborhood — the ethnic group to which the character Marie in my film belongs, as one of the two friends at the hair salon — the Taliban gave families 72 hours to leave their homes. Homes that they had bought, and that are now being confiscated from them. This shows that the persecution of the Hazara also continues with complete impunity. Not a day goes by that I don’t follow what is happening there.

I have a deep love for this wounded country and its people. Afghanistan is a country of many ethnic groups, some of which try to crush the others while defending themselves against outside aggression, all in the most brutal poverty. Yolande Zauberman ended her documentary M with a quotation from Franz Kafka: “I am among my own with a knife to protect them, I am among my own with a knife to attack them.” I think I could have borrowed it too.

Barbara Broccoli Appointed President Of UK’s National Film & Television School

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: Barbara Broccoli has been named the first female president of the UK’s National Film and Television School (NFTS).

The storied James Bond producer becomes President just a few weeks before the opening of a building dedicated to her father, the legendary Cubby Broccoli.

Barbara Broccoli will build on her longstanding relationship with the NFTS and commitment to championing emerging filmmaking talent.

She succeeds David Puttnam, another iconic British producer who takes on the honorary role of President Emeritus in recognition of his outstanding contribution to the NFTS and its continued success. Related Stories News Pippa Harris & YouTube UK Boss Alison Lomax Join Board Of National Film & Television School News The Sidemen & NFTS Team To Train Next Generation Of Unscripted Producers In UK

Broccoli is one of the industry’s most influential producers. She and her brother Michael G. Wilson were given an Honorary Academy Award in 2024. A year later she handed creative control of the iconic James Bond spy franchise to Amazon MGM Studios. The world is now eagerly anticipating Amazon’s announcement over who will play the next 007. Watch on Deadline

The NFTS’ Cubby Broccoli Building at its Beaconsfield campus will open in a few weeks. Officially named by Barbara Broccoli earlier this year in honour of her late father, the building will provide teaching facilities, creative spaces and the school’s first on-site student accommodation.

Broccoli said: “It’s a tremendous honour to become President of the National Film and Television School, following in Lord David Puttnam’s footsteps. Having been involved with the NFTS for many years, I have seen first-hand the extraordinary talent it develops and the vital role it plays in shaping the future of our industry. With the opening of The Cubby Broccoli Building taking place in January, this is especially meaningful. My father believed in the power of filmmaking and in giving talented people the opportunity to succeed.”

NFTS chair Sophie Turner Laing welcomed the “landmark moment” and thanked Puttnam, “whose support, wisdom and unwavering belief in the NFTS have been invaluable.”

Last month, British producer Pippa Harris and YouTube UK boss Alison Lomax joined the NFTS board.

Netflix to Release Documentary ‘Russell Brand: Open Secret’

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix will release a new documentary, Russell Brand: Open Secret, on Nov. 13. The film, directed by Olivia Isaacs, “charts the story of Russell Brand’s meteoric rise and steep fall following allegations of sexual offenses.”

According to a press release, Open Secret features inside voices and archive footage that offer insight into Brand’s life and the investigation run by U.K. publications the Times and the Sunday Times and their reporters Rosamund Urwin and Charlotte Wace. A short teaser for the film calls Brand a “shapeshifter” and a “grifter,” and explains that the journalists initially didn’t realize their investigation would lead to criminal charges.

Brand was found guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault on Nov. 5 following a trial in London. He was found not guilty on two additional counts of rape, as well as one count of attempted indecent assault. The allegations against him were tied to claims made by six women between 1999 and 2009. The actor and comedian was sentenced to 10 years in prison.

Police began investigating Brand in 2023 after multiple women came forward with allegations of assault and abuse in a Channel 4 documentary and the Sunday Times report. Prosecutors first filed five charges against Brand in April 2025, while two additional charges were brought in December.

Following the trial, Brand’s attorney Ian Winter confronted Urwin outside the courtroom, telling her “I hope you are pleased with yourself.” Urwin responded, “I’m pleased justice has been done,” to which Winter replied, “Well, it wasn’t. You fucked it.” Winter later confirmed he had referred himself to the Bar Standards Board.

Urwin explained on a Times podcast that she approached Winter after the verdict because her name had been mentioned during the trial. “I thought he’s talked about me a lot, I’ll go and introduce myself and that is a legitimate thing to do,” she said. “I was surprised to be confronted and spoken to in that way.”

The bulk of Brand’s sentence is tied to the rape charge, for which the judge sentenced him to nine years in prison. Meanwhile, each of the three assault charges carried their own penalty: nine months, 18 months, and eight months. The assault sentences will be served concurrently, but consecutively with the sentence for the rape charge. Trending Stories The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Sade Are Not Attending Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony He Thinks Malaysia Airlines Flight 370 Was Teleported. Thousands of People Believe Him Bob Dylan’s Meeting With Pope Leo: People Fixated on the Hood, But the Hands Spoke Volumes

Brand will eventually be eligible for parole, but only after serving two-thirds of his sentence. That means he will spend at least seven years behind bars, and, if granted parole, would have to serve the remainder of his sentence under supervision and abiding by specific rules.

The Metropolitan Police are continuing to investigate Brand after several people contacted them with information during and after the trial. “Detectives are assessing this information as part of their ongoing enquiries,” a spokesperson told the BBC.

Russell Brand Documentary From ‘Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story’ Producers Set at Netflix Days After Rape Sentencing

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Netflix has unveiled a documentary on Russell Brand, days after the comedian and former actor received a prison term of 10 years and six months for rape and sexual assault.

“Russell Brand: Open Secret” is directed by Olivia Isaacs and produced by Fremantle-owned 72 Films, the company behind “Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story.” The film follows Brand from his rapid ascent in comedy to his sharp fall after allegations of sexual offenses, through to his criminal trial. No premiere date was announced. Related Stories Sandra Hüller Makes Surprise Move to Supporting for 'Fatherland,' Setting Up Possible Double Oscar Nom With 'Rose' (EXCLUSIVE) Oscars: Best Original Score — Could One of Hollywood’s Most Overlooked Composers Finally Get His Moment?

It draws on insiders and archive material to examine Brand’s life. It also tracks the investigation carried out by The Times and The Sunday Times, led by the papers’ chief news correspondent Rosamund Urwin and investigations correspondent Charlotte Wace.

A London jury found Brand guilty of one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault. He was acquitted of two other rape charges and one charge of attempted indecent assault. Judge Bennathan handed down the sentence on Monday and ruled that Brand will not be eligible for release until he has completed two thirds of the term.

“About 20 years ago, Russell Brand, you were becoming famous,” the judge said in his sentencing remarks. “You were clever and, in many people’s eyes, funny and charismatic. You had also become a sexual predator.”

After the trial ended, the Metropolitan Police confirmed that Brand is under investigation over further rape allegations from two other women.

The allegations against Brand first came to light in 2023 in a docuseries made by The Sunday Times with Channel 4’s “Dispatches.” Until then, Brand was known chiefly for his comedy and for film roles in “Forgetting Sarah Marshall” and “Get Him to the Greek.”

Clare Cameron, David Glover and Mark Raphael are executive producers on “Open Secret.” Kate Yandle, Laura Spence and Felicity Cross produce.

Glover and Raphael founded 72 Films as an independent outfit focused on high-end documentaries, and the company is part of the Fremantle group. Its credits include “9/11: One Day in America,” which won an Emmy, and “John Lennon: Murder Without a Trial,” a Critics Choice Award winner. It also made the award-winning “The Zelensky Story” and “The Tony Blair Story.”

Arnold Schwarzenegger Calls for Democrats and Republicans to ‘Work Together’

📅 Nov 12, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Arnold Schwarzenegger stopped by Jimmy Kimmel Live to promote his new film, The Man With the Bag, and took the opportunity to reflect on being a Republican in today’s political climate.

Kimmel asked the movie star and politician if he is still a Republican early on in their conversation. “Yeah I’m a Republican, absolutely,” Schwarzenegger confirmed, explaining that a “traditional Republican is someone that admires the Republican party for having free the slaves.”

“This is the history,” he explained of the party. “This is what I’m about. Someone that has given those slaves American citizenship and has given them the right to vote is Republican.”

“This sounds like all of the opposite things the Republican party stands for now though, doesn’t it?” Kimmel responded.

“It doesn’t mean I have to change my philosophy,” Schwarzenegger said. “I have my philosophy. The Ronald Reagan philosophy, who created the air resources board in California so we can regulate our own air and reduce our greenhouse gases and pollution in California. That was done by Ronald Reagan, by a Republican. Nixon created the EPA. … So those are the kind of Republicans that used to go and work together with the Democrats and got things done. This is the kind of Republican that I am.”

“Unfortunately I think you’re the only one of them left,” Kimmel noted.

“No, I don’t think I’m the only one left,” Schwarzenegger acknowledged. “But this doesn’t mean that we should give up. The bottom line is we have just seen now what some people call the blue wave. I call it a tidal wave or a tsunami. It’s not really a wave anymore. But the bottom line of that is that now the Democrats go in there and they have the majority. And then the Republicans will go in there and they have the majority. And this is how it goes back and forth and back and forth.”

He added, “And it has been going back and forth like this for the last 20 years. And what happened in the last 20 years? None of them did true immigration reform. So we are still fighting over immigration. None of them participated in reducing our debt. None of them created a health care system where where everyone is insured.” Trending Stories The ‘You Can See Everything’ Interview You’ve Been Waiting For Sade Are Not Attending Rock & Roll Hall of Fame Induction Ceremony He Thinks Malaysia Airlines Flight 370 Was Teleported. Thousands of People Believe Him Bob Dylan’s Meeting With Pope Leo: People Fixated on the Hood, But the Hands Spoke Volumes

Schwarzenegger explained that if one party tries to do something by themselves it’s “bogus.” “What you have to do is the Democrats and the Republicans have to work together like we did when I was governor of California,” he said. “This is the only way you can accomplish something rather than always pointing fingers and accusing each other of things and calling each other names. No. You have to get together and accomplish and be a public servant rather than a party servant. There’s a difference.”

Elsewhere in the interview, Schwarzenegger reflected on Donald Trump asking for his support in 2016 when he initially ran for president. The former California governor explained that he felt he couldn’t endorse Trump’s campaign because they disagreed about certain issues, including climate change and pollution.
