‘Rivals’ Star David Tennant & EP Dominic Treadwell-Collins Break Down Season 2 Part 2’s Explosive First 3 Episodes: Grief, Scandal, Scheming & Heartbreak

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

SPOILER ALERT: The story includes details about Season 2 Episodes 7, 8 and 9 of Hulu‘s Rivals, which is based on Jilly Cooper’s 1988 novel of the same name.

Part 2 of Hulu’s Rivals picked up in the immediate aftermath of the Part 1 finale which ended with ruthless TV executive Tony’s (David Tennant) long-suffering wife Monica (Claire Rushbrook) dying in a car crash during a storm as she was on her way to tell Tony’s rival Declan (Aidan Turner) about her husband’s affair with his actress wife Maud (Victoria Smurfit). What’s more, Monica died in the hands of Tony’s mortal enemy Rupert (Alex Hassell) who got to deliver the news to him. Related Stories Casting Tony McNamara's 'Mosquito' Comedy Series Adds 10 To Cast As Production Begins In UK News Hulu Launches True-Crime Docuseries 'The Well' From Fox Entertainment Studios

Tony spent the opening episode of Part 2 reeling from Monica’s tragic death while facing the disdain of his own son Archie (Louis Landau) whose discovery of his father’s affair with Maud became the final straw for Monica to seek a divorce and go on that dangerous drive in the storm. Watch on Deadline

Following — quite literally — the Chekhov’s gun dramatic principle, the episode begins with Tony holding a rifle, which he used to chase away mourners attending the post-funeral reception at his house before firing it at the end, killing Monica’s beloved peacocks.

In a typical Rivals fashion, the somber funeral scene, featuring Archie breaking down while eulogizing his mom, was followed by sex romps involving two of the show’s couples in secret relationships, Lizzie (Katherine Parkinson) and Freddie (Danny Dyer), and Charles (Gary Lamont) and Gerald (Hubert Burton).

Taking place in the shadow of the AIDS epidemic, the 1987-set season saw Gerald, a newly minted Conservative MP, come out as gay to his aristocratic fiancée Muffy (Eliot Salt) who, after the initial shock, opted to proceed with the engagement as long as Gerald broke things off with Charles — which he did, leaving Charles devastated.

In the same very eventful episode, Tony told Maud he loved her. And, after almost everyone else had figured it out, Declan finally found out about their affair and made a spectacle of himself, chasing and accidentally knocking over Maud at the bonfire for all the society to see.

Rupert and Taggie (Bella Maclean) continued their will they-won’t they dance, with Taggie’s latest outreach at the end of Episode 9 rebuffed by Rupert with the argument that he wouldn’t be able to make her happy for long. And the Concorde made another appearance as Rupert and Declan flew to New York to bring Cameron (Nafessa Williams) back after falsely accusing her of being Tony’s mole at their Venturer TV venture.

Speaking to Deadline, Rivals star Tennant and writer/executive producer Dominic Treadwell-Collins discussed some of the biggest developments in Episodes 7, 8 and 9.

Check back after the Dec. 2 Season 2 finale for Part 2 of Deadline’s interview which breaks down the ending and how it sets up the previously greenlighted 12-episode Season 3, the season arcs of Rupert & Taggie and Freddie & Izzie as well as Perdita, whom Declan was not too pleased to meet in Episode 9 having burnt the Rupert kompromat about her in Season 1.

DEADLINE: Monica’s death has changed the paradigm and pushed Tony to the brink. Why did you make that change from the book where she is very much alive?

THREADWELL-COLLINS: That year in the UK, there was a massive storm at exactly this date, there was a genuine storm that changed the country a lot. Everyone at a certain age remembers the great storm, we still talk about it, so we always had to storyline that.

One of the things we always talked about in the writers’ room was there needed to be consequences for this rivalry. Otherwise, firstly, these silly men were going to keep going at each other, and actually, it needed to have some repercussions and some penance in the real world.

We talked a lot about a character dying, and we went through many many permutations. Monica was one my favorite characters, played by one of the best actresses in the show. She’s brilliant in Jilly’s book, but I’d also taken elements of my friend’s mother who I knew so well and loved and put her into it as well.

It felt like we had to lose someone utterly beloved. When we talked about killing Monica, I kept running away from it and saying, “Oh no, no, no! Let’s think about other characters.” And we did. We storylined with other characters in our heads, and we kept coming back to Monica. I think also because we have Claire Rushbrook, who in the UK has been known as one of our best actresses, playing Monica. If we didn’t use her, if we hadn’t done this, we would have had Monica turning a blind eye to Tony for the rest of the series going forward, and I never wanted to do that. I wanted to show everyone and new people, new audiences, just how brilliant Claire Rushbrook was.

THREADWELL-COLLINS: Monica’s death had to be painful. It had to cause Tony to act in the most terrible way. And truth be told, I didn’t know we were going to come back after Season 2, so I also went, Okay, this could be the last [season] of Rivals, let’s go blazing out into the sun.

No other character’s death would have been so painful; I pine for her and I miss her. And Laura Wade and I writing Episode 7, the funeral episode, we were really careful to pay tribute to Monica, to Claire Rushbrook, to do a funeral with elegance and dignity, and then also shake it up and move from George Michael’s Faith into Lizzie and Freddie bonking in a hotel room. We were really careful to try and calibrate it.

DEADLINE: Talk about using George Michael’s Faith for that transition and the series featuring so many 1980s hit songs.

THREADWELL-COLLINS: A lot of the songs are from the book, and when we write, all the writers, we have a playlist of music from that year. I always liked music from 1987 for this series, and then you also go back and look at some of the stuff in the 70s; it’s been really fun.

For me, the Eureka moment was Episode 7, realizing that George Michael’s Faith moves from organ music into that sexy beat and going, Oh gosh, that’s how we can segue between a funeral and a sex scene. A lot of music will inspire us when we’re writing. I think music’s such an important part of the show, it gives you all the feels and gives you that nostalgic feeling of being back in the 80s. Half the songs are things I heard in the back of my dad’s car when I was a kid and there’s something very comforting about that.

DEADLINE: There was a funny moment after the post-funeral sex, with Muffy trying to clean a sperm smudge off Gerald’s tie. Was that inspired by the hair-gel scene in There Is Something About Mary?

THREADWELL-COLLINS: No, that was not Something About Mary, I wish it was, but it wasn’t. Again, Laura and I were trying to make sure that we treated the funeral like another Rivals party. How does Rivals do a funeral? It was important to have some guffaws from the audience. And that moment, I phoned Laura and said, “Look, what about there being some sperm on Gerald’s tie?” and we then started playing around with it.

Laura and I will often act out bits of it together over the phone or on a Zoom, and it made us laugh. And Alex Lamb, our other exec, he then jumped in as well and said, “Okay, but if Muffy then licks her finger as you would do that. We all took it and played with it because tonally, Episode 7 was important in reintroducing everyone to Rutshire, paying homage to Monica, but also reminding an audience: you’re still going to laugh at this show. We’re not going to be completely somber.

DEADLINE: Tony is consumed by grief after Monica’s death. But did he love her or was he just mad about losing something he owns?

TENNANT: Probably both of the above. There isn’t any question that he loved her. I think she was his anchor, she was his home space, she was his touchstone. He absolutely took her for granted, and he treated her appallingly. But he needed her, and for a man who doesn’t have much of a moral compass within himself, I think she provided that for him. She probably limited his excesses, probably kept him on an equilibrium that he now does not have, which is not great for Tony going forward, I don’t think that’s going to play out very well.

DEADLINE: Monica’s peacocks did look doomed from the start, knowing that the gun Tony had been holding would eventually go off.

TENNANT: Did you think they were on a they were on a shaky nail from the off?

DEADLINE: I did. Still, it was a little extreme. Why did he do that?

TENNANT: To lose your life partner is devastating. Even for someone who’s not always the most morally engaged human, I think it’s bewildering and it’s distressing for him. He’s rudderless without her, so I think it’s quite a plausible reaction. He’s a man who doesn’t have a particularly well-oiled path to his own emotions, so I think the fact that such a traumatic moment should end with some kind of violent exorcism makes perfect sense. I think that’s who Tony is, unfortunately.

DEADLINE: But if he killed a person at that moment…

TENNANT: Then we would have had a very different story for Part 2 of [Season 2] but I think we should feel, and I think we do feel that in that moment it could easily have happened. Perhaps it takes his daughters on the stairs to finally make him see, to have a moment of objectivity as to what he’s doing, because he’s in a very bad place. Ultimately, what he does in that very English way is, as the episodes go forward, he sort of swallows that down, but that doesn’t mean it goes away. So there’s an acid in his stomach perhaps that is even worse than before.

DEADLINE: Monica did predict that Tony would become even more dangerous after her death but if he loved her even a little, why did he completely dismiss her dying wish for truce with Rupert, telling his rival that now he doesn’t have anything else left but fight?

TENNANT: Because that’s ultimately who he is. Monica, in many ways, was probably a limiting factor on him, so there’s something for him rather liberating about not having her there. That doesn’t mean it’s a good thing by anyone’s reckoning, but in terms of his need to mitigate his own excesses, that’s kind of been erased by Monica leaving him. So he’s unhinged. The anchor has gone, and I suppose also if losing a wife is in some sort of very animal way a kind of a loss, then his need for victory elsewhere becomes even more energized.

DEADLINE: Tony, clearly jealous of Maud and Declan’s reconciliation, tells her he loves her at Bonfire Night. Does he love her or is it about hurting Declan? We didn’t get a clear sense.

TENNANT: Well, I think that’s very human, isn’t it? I don’t think you necessarily will get a clear sense, I’m not sure that he has a clear sense. I think, in that moment of trauma and tumult, he will say whatever he needs to say to win that particular battle. And if that is difficult for Declan, then that’s not something that he will be shy of either. I think Tony is driven by animal instincts in every way, and whatever it takes to win the prey in the moment is what he will employ. That doesn’t necessarily mean he doesn’t mean it but whether that really means that Maud is the love of his life, I’m not sure. He may be too psychopathic to know that himself.

DEADLINE: Tony did tell Declan in Episode 9, “I didn’t want Maud, I just wanted to win.”

TENNANT: I think all his motivations, whether they are carnal or professional, they’re all mixed up and they’re all fluid and they’re all just part of the churn that’s inside this at this moment pretty unhinged individual.

DEADLINE: When Declan finally found out about Maud’s affair with Tony, he stopped himself from hitting her inside before chasing her outside. What prompted him to publicly chastise her. Was it just because she and Tony had had sex in the house?

THREADWELL-COLLINS: They had sex in the house. It was his worst possible enemy. It is Bonfire Night, and Declan’s had a few drinks. And also, Declan is a dangerous man as well as Tony is dangerous. Declan is dangerous, and we wanted to show that he pulls himself back from that moment. We made sure of that, but we also wanted to be a bit feral. I think what’s been really important with this story is in Jilly’s book.

In the original book, Tony and Maud have an affair, but it’s revealed at the end of the book. You don’t understand the journey, and because we had David Tennant, Aidan Turner, Victoria Smurfit, we wanted to see that play out and understand it. And in the book, Declan forgives her quite easily. We really wanted to play that out against Bonfire Night, it was perfect. It’s November, and it’s also a strange British celebration festival that the Americans don’t have, where we celebrate someone trying to burn down Parliament. It is bizarre.

DEADLINE: Rivals introduced a gay love story that is not in the book with Charles and Gerald. Can you talk about that creative decision and the duo’s heartbreaking Episode 8 break-up scene?

THREADWELL-COLLINS: It’s always been important to me in everything I do to tell queer stories as a gay man myself. In the book, Charles and Gerald don’t even cross, I don’t think, they’re two characters who exist in Jilly’s world.

It was important to us to have a gay love story that has as much weight as the straight love stories, and have the audience rooting for Charles and Gerald. But we also need to be aware of the fact that this isn’t all sunshine and rainbows. It’s 1987, and Charles and Gerald couldn’t easily be together. You’ve got the shadows of AIDS around us as well, and we talked about being as true as possible.

I mean, we put Charles and Gerald through the wringer, and hopefully get some joy at the end of the series. But we’ve got to be really aware of the the social context of the story, while also having audiences who maybe wouldn’t have watched a gay love story before start rooting for them. I think that’s the power of television to change the world a bit.

Zac Taylor: Duke Tobin is "really apologetic" after backing out of trade with Eagles

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

On Tuesday, the Bengals backed out of a trade for edge rusher Arnold Ebiketie after it was revealed that the deal might cost Cincinnati with a future compensatory draft pick.

During his Wednesday press conference, head coach Zac Taylor explained the situation from the perspective of Bengals director of player personnel — and de facto General Manager — Duke Tobin.

“Obviously, we tried something yesterday,” Taylor said. “Duke was very explicit with me when he came and spoke to me about how he wanted this portrayed. I’m not comfortable doing it, but I’m going to do what he asked. We did make an effort to put some depth in there in place of Cashius [Howell]. As we got into it, it was going to be more costly than we initially anticipated.

“He’s really apologetic about it and wants it made clear — Philly was a great trade partner. It was on our end. Certainly, we have a ton of confidence in our front office and how they do things. They’ve given us the players that have allowed us to be 6-2 right now, and playing at such a high level. So, we’ll put that behind us and move forward, and just get excited for this game on Sunday. Our locker room is in a great place right now.”

Taylor noted Tobin didn’t really tell him how everything went down and he didn’t ask the question.

“It doesn’t really matter to me. It probably took three minutes of my day yesterday away from watching tape,” Taylor said. “So, got a lot of faith in how those guys operate and we’ll just move forward with it.”

With that, the Bengals didn’t make any deadline moves to improve the team. Taylor said he and Tobin like where the team is.

“I think you can talk to the players in the locker room, they really like where we’re at,” Taylor said. “So, this is a team with a lot of confidence right now. I know it’s a fun period of time — the weeks leading up to the trade deadline — for fans … but the truth is, we really like where we’re at. Our guys are playing with a high degree of confidence and we’ll continue to move forward.”

Marvel Officially Retcons Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom MCU Timeline

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Doctor Doom has returned to the Marvel Cinematic Universe a month before Avengers: Doomsday, and the latest appearance of Robert Downey Jr.'s new MCU character retcons the villain's timeline in the franchise. Doctor Doom is one of the most powerful characters in Marvel Comics, and with magic and technology at his side in December's Avengers movie, he will be so too in the MCU.

After all, we have already seen Doctor Doom easily send Thor flying in Avengers: Doomsday's trailers. However, in terms of substance, Doctor Doom has not had many MCU appearances outside the footage for the upcoming Marvel movie. That was to be expected, given how the shared universe was originally set on Kang the Conqueror as the Multiverse Saga's Thanos replacement.

The pivot to Downey as Doctor Doom came late in the game, following Jonathan Majors' MCU firing, which meant that the character was entirely absent from the early phases of this multi-year plan, different from Kang and his multiverse variants, who had been set up for the scrapped Avengers: The Kang Dynasty through appearances in Loki and Ant-Man and the Wasp: Quantumania.

Rearrange the movies into the correct US release order.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Warning! This article contains SPOILERS for VisionQuest episode 8.That said, Marvel Studios has since actively started to release new Doctor Doom appearances through post-credits scenes in multiple MCU releases to establish his franchise presence ahead of Avengers: Doomsday. The latest of those came in a surprise VisionQuest post-credits scene that even the press did not get to watch beforehand, and the sequence retconned Doctor Doom's MCU timeline.

Before the VisionQuest finale was released on Disney+, Downey's Doctor Doom had appeared in The Fantastic Four: First Steps, which is not set in the main MCU Earth, and made his Earth-616 debut in one of Avengers: Endgame Encore's post-credits scenes, specifically the one where he showed up to take Mark Ruffalo's Hulk away following the events of Spider-Man: Brand New Day. That was initially thought to be the first time Doctor Doom appeared on Earth-616 in the MCU, as the Fantastic Four movie teased through its setup of Latveria and its ruler that Downey's Victor von Doom hails from that universe.

Then came the VisionQuest finale, which shockingly had Downey return as Doctor Doom to resurrect Elizabeth Olsen's Scarlet Witch. Judging by how Marvel Studios presented the scene, using footage from Wanda Maximoff's death in Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness to then have Doctor Doom lift the ruins of Mount Wundagore, which fell on top of Wanda, and resurrect the character, it seems like Doom saved Elizabeth Olsen's MCU hero right after the events of the Doctor Strange sequel. This retcons his presence in the MCU and Earth-616, making his first appearance on that Earth set earlier in the MCU timeline.

Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness happened shortly after Spider-Man: No Way Home, with the movie being set in 2024 in the MCU timeline. Spider-Man: Brand New Day is set four years after No Way Home, with Peter Parker living alone and sad as Ned and MJ graduate MIT. As such, instead of his first MCU Earth-616 appearance being set in 2028, as Doctor Doom got Bruce Banner after the events of Tom Holland's latest MCU movie, Downey's Marvel villain was already visiting the main Earth of the shared universe back in 2024 in the MCU timeline, a VisionQuest retcon.

Now, there are two key ways in which that could have happened. One of those is that Downey's Doctor Doom mastered multiversal time travel back in 2024 and slipped in and out of Earth-616 without anyone knowing, resurrecting Wanda and taking her to his reality, which is why Rio Vidal, aka Death, said Wanda was gone in Agatha All Along. The other is that, like Downey's other character, Tony Stark/Iron Man, Doctor Doom cracked time travel, so he manages to go back in time to resurrect Wanda after arriving on Earth-616 following Spider-Man: Brand New Day. VisionQuest's post-credits scene sets up many Doctor Doom theories ahead of Avengers: Doomsday.

Dolphins make Josh Uche a team captain after three other captains go on injured reserve

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Before the season, the Dolphins named six team captains. Three of those six are now on injured reserve, which left an opening that defensive end Josh Uche will fill.

Dolphins head coach Jeff Hafley announced today that Uche was chosen after consulting with the other team captains, who felt Uche was deserving of wearing the C on his jersey.

“They came to me and they wanted to name another captain, so I listened, and we named Josh Uche a captain today,” Hafley said. “A guy who has really done a tremendous job on and off the field. He is another guy who has been an outstanding leader for our young guys. He’s a guy who last year toward the end of the season was not even active, and now he’s a guy that people have to game plan around. He’s playing the run well, getting his pressures and his sacks, and he’s been an unbelievable teammate and great help to me.”

Dolphins quarterback Malik Willis, running back De’Von Achane and defensive lineman Zach Sieler are all captains who are now on injured reserve. In addition to Uche, the captains still on the active roster are left tackle Patrick Paul, center Aaron Brewer and linebacker Jordyn Brooks.

Fate of 10/10 ‘Friends’ Replacement Officially Confirmed With Massive Update

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Charlie Cox will always be known for his role as Daredevil, first in the Netflix Marvel series before later reprising the role in Daredevil: Born Again, but he’s also stumbled into other roles in film and TV that you may recognize. Last year, Cox provided the voice work for Gustave in the critically acclaimed game, Clair Obscur: Expedition 33, and he’s confirmed to various outlets over the last year that he’s working on another video game that will likely be announced soon. Back in 2022, when fans were still campaigning for Kevin Feige and other bosses for Marvel to Save Daredevil, Cox took his talents to Netflix to star in one of the platform’s most underrated thrillers, Treason. The mini-series consisted of only five episodes, but fans still demand to this day that it be brought back for another season.

Last year, Cox featured in a few episodes of the hit FX comedy series, Adults, as Mr. Teacher. His role was sparse, true, but he was a fan-favorite addition to the show, which is led by stars such as Malik Elsasal and Lucy Freyer. After a successful first season, FX renewed Adults for a second season, which dropped as a binge on Hulu at the end of August. Charlie Cox did not return in Season 2. The future of Adults had been unclear as FX weighed what to do about the series, but the studio announced this afternoon that the show has been officially renewed for Season 3. Better yet, Adults Season 3 has received an increased episode order to 16, which is more than the eight episodes in the first and second season. This comes after the second season improved in the viewership column by upwards of 60%.

Four love stories, four completely different ideas of what it means to be somebody's person. This is the one that matches how you actually fall, and what it would ask of you.

You do not fall for strangers. You fall for the person you have already been talking to for years, which is the slowest and by some distance the most durable way anybody has found to do this.

You want a partner, not an audience. Somebody who wants their own life as badly as you want yours, and who would never ask you to be smaller to make the relationship easier.

The intensity was never the problem. The secrecy is. You are drawn to a love that lives entirely in private, and to the particular ache of wanting somebody you are not supposed to want.

You do not do this cautiously. When it happens for you it is immediate and total and it rearranges everything, and you would not accept a smaller version of it.

The first season of Adults earned a solid 77% from critics on Rotten Tomatoes, but it still did well enough to warrant a renewal. Season 2 was a massive hit, though, earning a rare, perfect 100% score from critics on the aggregate site. The hit sitcom has often been compared to Friends, and while there are some notable differences between the shows, fans of one are almost guaranteed to enjoy the other. The official synopsis for the show, which is expected to return before the end of next year, reads as follows:

“Adults is an ensemble comedy series about a group of twenty-somethings in New York trying to be good people, despite being neither "good" nor "people" yet. Samir, Billie, Paul Baker, Issa and Anton are five friends crashing together in Samir's childhood home, where they share their meals, anxieties and, occasionally, toothbrushes. The show puts a slightly heightened twist on the wins, losses and humiliations of starting out in the adult world. Whether they're trying to get ahead at work, navigating the healthcare system, hosting a dinner party or dating in the age of Find My Friends, the group is finding that nothing about the real world is simple, and all their best intentions tend to make things worse. These friends are works-in-progress, brimming with bad ideas and whatever their latest "theory of adulthood" might be. But there's one thing they consistently get right: showing up for each other.”

Check out the first two seasons of Adults on Hulu and stay tuned to Collider for more updates and coverage of the future of the show.

Santa Barbara Film Fest: Anne Hathaway, Star of ‘The Odyssey,’ Tapped for American Riviera Award

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The Oscar-winning actress will be feted on Feb. 5 with an evening of clips and conversation.

Anne Hathaway, the Oscar-winning actress who is back in the awards conversation for her portrayal of Penelope, opposite Matt Damon’s Odysseus, in Christopher Nolan’s acclaimed blockbuster The Odyssey, has been tapped by the Santa Barbara International Film Festival to receive its American Riviera Award.

Hathaway will be feted with an evening of clips and conversation, moderated by yours truly, at Santa Barbara’s historic Arlington Theatre on the evening of Friday, Feb. 5.

“Anne Hathaway has always impressed me with her theatrical sophistication, unwavering commitment, and remarkable versatility as a performer,” SBIFF executive director Roger Durling said in a statement. “In The Odyssey, she delivers a breathtaking performance, one that stands among the finest of her career, channeling simmering rage and profound emotional complexity into a portrayal that is at once fierce, vulnerable, poignant and unforgettable.” Related Stories Movies Christopher Nolan's 'The Odyssey' Will Return to Theaters Next Month as Awards Season Heats Up Movies Robert Pattinson to Be Honored With American Cinematheque Award

The American Riviera Award was created by SBIFF to celebrate actors who have made a significant contribution to American cinema. Previous recipients include Ethan Hawke, Zoe Saldaña, Mark Ruffalo, Brendan Fraser, Kristen Stewart, Delroy Lindo, Renée Zellweger, Viggo Mortensen, Sam Rockwell, Jeff Bridges, Michael Keaton, Rachel McAdams, Patricia Arquette, Robert Redford, Quentin Tarantino, Martin Scorsese, Annette Bening, Sandra Bullock, Mickey Rourke, Tommy Lee Jones, Forrest Whitaker, Philip Seymour Hoffman, Kevin Bacon and Diane Lane.

Hathaway is known for her versatility as a performer and producer. Her filmography includes beloved comedies like 2001’s The Princess Diaries and 2006’s The Devil Wears Prada; and acclaimed dramas like 2005’s Brokeback Mountain, 2008’s Rachel Getting Married and 2012’s Les Misérables, the last of which brought her best supporting actress Oscar, Golden Globe, SAG and BAFTA awards.

In addition to The Odyssey — which marks her third collaboration with Nolan, following 2012’s The Dark Knight Rises and 2014’s Interstellar — her remarkable 2026 has also included the films Mother Mary, The Devil Wears Prada 2, The End of Oak Street and Verity, an adaptation of Colleen Hoover’s bestselling novel.

Hathaway’s upcoming projects include Ron Howard’s Alone at Dawn, Days of Thunder 2 opposite Tom Cruise, and The Princess Diaries 3.

Through her production company, Somewhere Pictures, Hathaway has also produced projects including The Idea of You, Verity and Mother’s Instinct.

The 42nd Santa Barbara International Film Festival will take place Feb. 3-13, 2027.

Matt LaFleur "shocked" Vikings acquired Kirk Cousins for only a third-round pick

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Packers head coach Matt LaFleur was Kirk Cousins’ position coach when the quarterback came into the league.

Now the two are set to match up on opposite sidelines again after the Raiders traded Cousins to the Vikings on Tuesday.

LaFleur told reporters in his Wednesday press conference that he’s expecting Cousins to start Sunday’s matchup between Minnesota and Green Bay.

“Yeah, he’ll be out there. He’ll be starting. Fairly confident in that,” LaFleur said. “Obviously, he’s got familiarity with their system. Even if he didn’t, he’s a really intelligent guy and I think he’s been playing at a high level.”

What did make LaFleur raise an eyebrow was the trade compensation, as Minnesota sent a third-round pick to Las Vegas for Cousins.

“I was surprised they didn’t get more for him, quite honestly — I was shocked what they traded him for,” LaFleur said. “It was mind-blowing to me.”

While Cousins played under Vikings head coach Kevin O’Connell in 2023 and 2024, LaFleur said what Cousins has been doing with the Raiders this year still has relevance when putting together this week’s game plan.

“You definitely watch some of that, see what he’s had success with, what he’s comfortable with,” LaFleur said. “Obviously, he’s got a long track record with that offense with all those years in Minnesota with Kevin. And [there are still] a lot of the key pieces that were there with him.”

In nine games for the Raiders this year, Cousins completed 64.1 percent of his passes for 2,340 yards with 20 touchdowns and eight interceptions.

Popcorn at The Paris Theater and Cocktails at Bergdorf’s: Inside Netflix’s Avedon Screening and After-Party

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Richard Avedon, the revered photographer whose work has helped shape the legacy of Vogue, had a choice way of introducing himself: “I can’t write, I can’t type, I can’t read my own handwriting, but I can see like a son of a b*tch.” These are the very words uttered by the late lensman as he introduces himself in a new Netflix documentary chronicling his life.

Ahead of its official streaming release on November 11, Ron Howard’s highly anticipated Avedon premiered at the Paris Theater, Manhattan’s longest-running arthouse cinema. Guests dutifully lined up upon arrival at the 58th Street institution, eager to pick up butter-topped popcorn and sour candy before securing their seats.

Greatly influenced by film and dance, Avedon’s genre-defining artistic direction saw him remove models from the confines of studio backdrops and instead photograph them in everyday settings. “He was smart and he was also the right talent and individual for the right time, as it relates to the mediums of photography and magazines,” shared Howard while discussing the project with The New Yorker’s Adam Gopnik on stage.

Attendees settled in to watch the 106-minute documentary which premiered at the Cannes Film Festival earlier this year. During his six-decade career, Avedon lensed seminal ads for brands like Calvin Klein and Versace, and captured cultural figures of the day including Andy Warhol, Marilyn Monroe, Audrey Hepburn, the Beatles, and Jacqueline and John F. Kennedy. Later, he pivoted from fashion to hard news, capturing images of the Black Panther Party and the Chicago Seven, and even traveled to Saigon to document the impact of the Vietnam War.

“The process really did become a character study of this artist,” Howard said of the documentary. “Although Richard didn’t care passionately about art museums, he was unbelievably passionate about movies and theater. Overall, he really saw himself as a director.”

Following a heartfelt round of applause, the crimson-colored curtains closed, signaling it was time for the after-party to get underway. Thankfully, moviegoers only had to traipse as far right as next door to Bergdorf Goodman, sauntering past the newly installed winter wonderland decor.

On the department store’s second floor, Avedon photography books brimming with famous portraits of Liza Minnelli and Brooke Shields were given pride of place in between displays of Jimmy Choos and Christian Louboutins. Amid the leopard print carpets and chartreuse velvet sofas, guests swapped their favorite moments from the documentary over petite cones of tuna tartare and tangy apple-ginger cocktails.

‘Adults’ Renewed for Season 3 at FX

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The third season of the ensemble comedy will consist of 16 episodes

“Adults” has been renewed for Season 3 at FX and Hulu, Variety has learned.

The third season of the ensemble comedy will consist of 16 episodes, which is double the eight episodes the show aired for both its first and second seasons. In addition, creators Ben Kronengold and Rebecca Shaw have also set a new overall deal with FX Productions.

“We started working with FX over six years ago, back when Paul Baker was but a twinkle in all of our eyes,” said Kronengold and Shaw. “In the years since, John [Landgraf] and the rest of the team have taught us everything we know about how to lead, produce, and tell stories that make people feel like part of a community. They’ve become family, and we’re so glad to make it official — and to start yelling at them like we do our parents.” Related Stories 'The Notebook' Musical Sets West End Run at Newly Renamed Judi Dench Theatre Connor Storrie in Talks to Play Villain Opposite Ryan Gosling in Marvel's 'Ghost Rider'

“Adults” stars Malik Elassal, Lucy Freyer, Jack Innanen, Amita Rao, and Owen Thiele. Per the official logline, the show is “about a group of twenty-somethings in New York leaning on each other to navigate love, work, friendship and family. Samir (Elassal), Billie (Freyer), Paul Baker (Innanen), Issa (Rao) and Anton (Thiele) are five friends crashing together in Samir’s childhood home.”

Kronengold and Shaw are executive producers and showrunners on the series in addition to creating it. Nick Kroll, Sarah Naftalis, Jonathan Krisel, Alicia Van Couvering, and Rob Rosell also executive produce. FX Productions is the studio.

“Ben and Rebecca have elevated ‘Adults’ in every way, earning critical praise and a legion of devoted fans who have embraced the friend group as their own,” said Nick Grad, president of FX Entertainment. “’Adults’ showed significant growth in its sophomore year and we’ve seen the show inspire everything from memes to Halloween costumes, so the decision was easy – more episodes and a new deal at FX for Ben and Rebecca!”

Per FX, Season 2 was up 60% in viewership over Season 1 on Hulu and Disney+ globally through its first five weeks, while international viewership was up 100%. FX did not provide specific viewership numbers, however.

“Adults” has been well-received by critics and audiences since its original launch in 2025, with FX also releasing the standalone special “Marathon Day” in July 2026 ahead of the debut of Season 2. The show currently has an 88% critical approval rating across both seasons on Rotten Tomatoes. In her review of Season 2 for Variety, Alison Herman wrote, “‘Adults’ finally has its own momentum, largely generated by a cast whose rapport obviates the need for any sweeping statements about their generation.”

Rose Byrne Goes Rosy Pink in Straight-Off-the-Runway Dior

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Rose Byrne is pretty in pink. Last night, the cast of crime-thriller series The Good Daughter gathered in Times Square for the New York City premiere. And for the occasion, the leading actor showed up to the buzzing area’s Times Center event space in a straight-off-the-runway gown from Jonathan Anderson’s Spring/Summer 2027 presentation for Dior.

On top, the baby-pink look was crafted from lightweight silk chiffon, which had been finely pleated into a textured peplum bodice with a sleeveless silhouette. The neckline and arm holes were trimmed with little ruffles, but at the hem, Anderson left things raw—giving a rough edge to an otherwise soft and delicate number.

From the flared tunic, airy pale-blush chiffon floated down to the ankle, creating a two-tone mid-length skirt that was finished with a second raw hem. On the runway, the piece was paired with cherry-red heels with white piping, but at the premiere, Byrne went with a simple pointed-toe white alternative.

When it came to jewelry, the Bridesmaids actor left her wrists and ears bare, selecting only a statement necklace with crystal-embellished leaf motifs. Her shoulder-length hair was blown out with a deep side part, and for makeup, she went rosy—with pink blush and warm coral-red lips.

Next to Byrne, costar Meghann Fahy went for an opposite look, wearing a silky black midi dress with a high neckline, three-quarter sleeves, and a slim fit. She added over-the-knee velvet boots and slicked her hair back into an updo while keeping her makeup simple.

Denis Villeneuve Talks ‘Dune: Part Three’ and Rebooting James Bond: ‘We Cannot F*ck It Up.’

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

“I’m out of a job,” Denis Villeneuve said, jokingly. Hours earlier, Villeneuve had signed off on the IMAX 70-milimeter print of Dune: Part Three, officially wrapping post-production on that film—and in turn, a decade-long chapter of the Quebecois writer-director’s working life.

Fresh off this milestone, Villeneuve and GQ had come to the Lucas Museum of Narrative Art in Los Angeles for a conversation and a private screening of Dune: Part Two, presented by OMEGA, in honor of his anointment as GQ’s Director of the Year. Global Editorial Director Adam Baidawi introduced Villeneuve before the director and GQ’s Zach Baron dove into a conversation that covered his original Dune plans, where Dune: Part Three breaks from its predecessors, and Villeneuve’s feelings about taking over the James Bond franchise.

GQ’s Adam Baidawi and Zach Baron with Denis Villeneuve

“I feel light,” the director of the year told Baron, then added, “Numb.” He was still processing Dune’s end, he said—while also coming to grips with closing the book on “the most rewarding, inspiring, and best ten years of my life as a filmmaker.”

Villeneuve’s first two Dune films have raked in more than a billion dollars at the box office. Part Three appears likely to nearly double that when it hits theaters (and locks up most of the world’s IMAX screens) a week before Christmas. But at one point during his conversation with Baron, the director pushed back gently on the notion of these movies as a “franchise.”

“There was nothing industrial in the way they were made,” Villeneuve said. They were the work of “a bunch of friends”—a familial crew, out in the desert putting things together.

“In French,” the Quebecois director continued, “there is a word—artisanal. They are very personal. Made with a lot of care. They’re little big things—but in a way, they are delicate.”

After all, as sprawling and cosmically grand as they are, these movies are also a deeply personal project for the director, born of images that took root in his head when he first read Frank Herbert’s thick-as-a-brick, notoriously adaptation-resistant 1965 novel Dune at age 13. Later, as Villeneuve racked up credits and clout, people would ask him what his dream project was; the answer was always Dune.

“I kept saying to myself, Someone else will do it one day,” he told Baron. “Another filmmaker will adapt this book again. And I kept waiting, and I kept waiting, until I felt ready to do it myself.”

Herbert wrote five escalatingly bonkers Dune sequels before his death in 1986, but Villeneuve intended to walk away from the table after adapting the first book across two films.

“Trilogies are very dangerous,” he told Baron. “The first movie is a promise, and if you fulfill the promise with the second one, then there’s a feeling of security that comes with that, and you feel that you have a recipe…That’s the trap. Because then you repeat yourself.”

But after wrapping Part Two, Villeneuve found himself unable to stop thinking about Dune Messiah—the second Dune novel, which Herbert supposedly wrote in frustration, believing the audience had missed the point by embracing Paul Atreides as a hero.

Villeneuve said his take on Messiah is “a different beast” than the first two films, built it around the tension between Timothée Chalamet’s Paul and his Fremen consort Chani (Zendaya), who Villeneuve has re-imagined—in a pivotal departure from Herbert’s version—as a nonbeliever who takes a critical view of Paul’s rise to power.

Neel Shah, Timothy Simons, and Michael Lasker

Of course, the specter (S.P.E.C.T.R.E.?) of another franchise hung over the proceedings at the Lucas Museum. Villeneuve, after all, is only technically between jobs; he’s a self-described “monomaniac” who insists that he can only hyper-focus on one project at a time and has been locked in on Dune for a decade, but the as-yet-untitled 26th Bond film is in motion, with Villeneuve signed on to direct.

GQ’s Adam Baidawi, Denis Villeneuve, and OMEGA’s Lucien Jornod

He could say almost nothing in public about the project, or even when he’d begin shooting it—he joked about having “the target of the sniper” on his chest—but spoke of feeling the responsibility not to fail: “We cannot fuck it up. We do not have the choice.”

Over the past week, the casting rumors around Villeneuve’s Bond reboot have reached what our UK colleagues accurately termed the “fever-dream” stage. It might be Callum Turner or Slow Horses’ Jack Lowden, but it might be Robert Pattinson, who’s emerged as a surprise front-runner after months of supposition about the part being earmarked for a relative unknown. At some point, toward the end of Tuesday’s conversation, the subject of Bond 26’s lead could no longer be avoided.

“Has the role of James Bond been cast?” Baron asked the director.

“It’s—it’s in the process. In the process,” Villeneuve said with a smile. “I am pushing the gas.”

“Blink once for Robert Pattinson,” Baron suggested, “and twice for Callum Turner.”

Villeneuve, for the record, did not blink. ✦

GQ would like to thank Legendary Entertainment, Warner Bros., and Brianna Smith.

Rocky’s Best Line Is Still the Greatest Quote in Franchise History

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sylvester Stallone’s 1976 Oscar-winner Rocky carved itself into Hollywood history thanks to its timeless tale of underdog spirit, and a catalog of memorable moments. Stallone’s script was filled with lines that are quoted to this day – the triumphant cry of “Adrian!," Mickey (Burgess Meredith) declaring, “You’re gonna eat lightnin’ and you’re gonna crap thunder!” or Apollo Creed (Carl Weathers) joking “Apollo Creed versus the Italian Stallion. Sounds like a damn monster movie.” There’s one quote, however, that stands out among the rest, one that sums up everything about Rocky Balboa, and the franchise that tells his story, is all about. It’s not said in the ring, or on top of the famous steps, but in one of the boxer’s most private moments.

The scene in question comes the night before the climactic fight against Creed. Rocky (Stallone) walks around the arena, then back home to his girlfriend Adrian (Talia Shire). Sitting on the edge of the bed, he quietly confesses that he can’t beat Apollo, before adding: “It really don't matter if I lose this fight. It really don't matter if this guy opens my head, either. 'Cause all I wanna do is go the distance. Nobody's ever gone the distance with Creed, and if I can go that distance, you see, and that bell rings, and I'm still standin', I'm gonna know for the first time in my life, see, that I weren't just another bum from the neighborhood.”

At that one moment, The Italian Stallion’s journey comes into perspective. He is a fighter from the street, going up against the heavyweight champion of the world. The odds are against him, and even he can’t see himself knocking out a man who is at the peak of a sport when he had only ever been a manager. However, there’s a way to win even if he loses. That speech becomes the key question of the movie, and every sequel that came after it: can you go the distance?

Anthony Ippolito's uncanny performance and a heartwarming script make this an unmissable film.

Rocky isn’t the story of the preparation for a boxing match. It is the story of personal triumph, of one man who seemed like he didn’t have a future, whose own trainer called his choices “a waste of life,” betting on himself to go toe-to-toe with the best in the world. It’s about becoming more than you thought you could be, pushing the limits of what people expect of you, and shocking the world not by winning or losing, but by going the distance.

It’s a mindset that has prevailed in the six Rocky movies. Rocky II, arriving in 1979, saw the boxer strive to prove the first fight wasn’t a fluke. The third film saw him made complacent by money and fame, having to recapture his underdog spirit when he comes up against a fighter as hungry as he used to be. The fourth film deals with personal grief and revenge, while the fifth sees Rocky turning away from his life’s work in order to be a father and husband. Even his final fight, in 2006’s Rocky Balboa, is less about stepping into the ring than it is proving he still has something to give. Whether on the top of the mountain or haunted by the past, going the distance was always the goal.

It’s easy to assume every Rocky movie ends in glorious victory, but some of the key moments in the franchise are moral victories where someone else’s hand is raised. The first film sees Apollo win on a points victory, with the press and fans surrounding the champion, while Rocky and Adrian embrace each other as if they had won, because the underdog went the distance. Rocky III ends with a victory, but it is defined by a loss, as Rocky underestimates Clubber Lang (Mr T.) and the resulting defeat reignites the spark. Rocky IV sees Apollo die in the ring, a devastating end to a defining relationship, while Rocky Balboa comes full circle as, now in his 60s, he still finds victory in defeat, going all 12 rounds with a much younger champion.

While he stood across formidable men in the ring, Rocky’s most formidable opponent was always himself. Going the distance meant overcoming flaws and demons and becoming who he knew he could be. In the first two Creed movies, where Rocky becomes the trainer of Adonis Creed (Michael B Jordan), the son of his old rival-turned-friend, he imparts that same wisdom onto his protégé. Making Adonis stand in front of a mirror, Rocky points to the reflection and says: “That's the toughest opponent you're ever going to have to face.”

It all comes back to that one moment in Rocky’s small apartment, and a speech that would encapsulate everything that matters about the world’s most famous boxer. Winning and losing are secondary to the personal mountains we have to climb, and the knowledge that we have gone the distance against the odds can be better than any championship.

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

WARNING: This article includes MAJOR spoilers from VisionQuest season 1, episode 8 finale.The Marvel Cinematic Universe just dropped its biggest twist for 2026.

Throughout its run since Iron Man kicked off the MCU timeline in 2008, the franchise has seen various heroes and villains connect with audience members worldwide, whether they are passionate comic book readers or casual moviegoers. While many characters have met grim fates over the many phases, the dead don't always stay dead.

The final seconds of the installment see Robert Downey Jr.'s Doctor Doom making his latest collection, as he is seen taking Elizabeth Olsen's Scarlet Witch, taking the audience back to her final moments in Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness, revealing that Wanda Maximoff is, in fact, alive.

With Victor von Doom uttering the words, "Scarlet Witch, awaken. Your powers will be mine," as she opens her eyes, it's clear that she will play some part in his massive plan in the December film.

Prince George’s Secret Talent Proves He’s Spreading His Wings

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Prince George put his artistic talents on display in the latest issue of Country Life magazine, which features a drawing of a kingfisher done by the 13-year-old.

Prince George is displaying a crowning achievement.

The eldest son of Prince William and Kate Middleton showed off his immense artist talent in an upcoming issue of Country Life magazine, guest-edited by his father, with a drawing of a kingfisher done by the 13-year-old.

George’s drawing features the bird with dark green and yellow plumage—and the signature pointed bill of a kingfisher—perched on top of a piece of wood, gazing off into the distance.

And he’s had a knack for the arts for years. Back in 2022, he drew a portrait of a reindeer that his family—including siblings Princess Charlotte, 11, and Prince Louis, 8—shared as their Christmas card on Instagram that year.

It's no wonder that he has picked up a palette, as both his mother and his grandfather King Charles III are quite artistically inclined as well. Kate received her degree in art history and has previously displayed her skills, posting a sketch of St. Andrews in Scotland in 2021 as well as photographing the royal family over the years. Charles, meanwhile, is known for his landscapes, often depicting Balmoral Castle in Scotland, Windsor Castle and other royal properties.

As for Kate, she shared a look at the whole family’s art skills in 2025, posting portraits she and her children had drawn to Instagram that had provided “a moment of connection”—although she did not say who had drawn which portrait.

In recent months George has been keeping busy not only with art but also as a student at Eton College while he prepares to one day take the throne following his grandfather and father.

"There’s lots of things to think about with that," William noted on an October episode of The Reluctant Traveler With Eugene Levy. "But obviously, I want to create a world in which my son is proud of what we do and a world and a job that actually does impact people’s lives for the better.”

And much of that desire comes from what he and brother Prince Harry experienced during their childhoods, adding, "That is caveated with I hope we don’t go back to some of the practices in the past that Harry and I had to grow up in. And I’ll do everything I can to make sure we don’t regress in that situation."

For a look at some of Kate and William’s sweet family moments, keep reading.

The Wales family's team of five consisting of Prince William, Kate Middleton, Prince George, Princess Charlotte and Prince Louis attended the August 2026 Commonwealth Games in Glasgow.

As a cancer survivor, the Princess of Wales took part in the National Three Peaks Challenge—climbing the three highest peaks in Scotland, England and Wales within 24 hours in June 2026—to support The Royal Marsden Cancer Charity. And her loved ones were there to show their support.

Per tradition, William, Kate and their kids joined royal family members in closing out the June 2026 Trooping the Colour ceremonial parade with a wave from the balcony of London's Buckingham Palace.

The Wales family hopped over to St. George's Chapel at Windsor Castle to celebrate Easter in April 2026.

Coordinating in navy and green attire, William, Kate, George, Charlotte and Louis got into the holiday spirit by attending the December 2025 Together at Christmas Carol Service at London's Westminster Abbey.

The couple and their two eldest children served up another sweet family moment at Wimbledon in July 2025.

Louis was captivated by the flypast he watched with his mom, dad and siblings from Buckingham Palace's balcony that took place on the 80th anniversary of VE Day in May 2025.

From singing "King of My Heart" to meeting the future kings of England! Taylor Swift and Travis Kelce snapped a selfie with William, George and Charlotte during one of her June 2024 London shows.

Fast-forward two years later, and William appeared on Travis' podcast New Heights, with the episode dropping the same day the Kansas City Chiefs tight end wed the Grammy winner in July 2026.

For Father's Day 2024, Kate shared this snap of William and their kids enjoying a day at the beach.

A then 5-year-old Louis sweetly stole the show during Trooping the Colour 2023.

It's Beginning to Look A Lot Like Christmas

The Wales family shared jolly smiles as they headed to Christmas service at Sandringham's St. Mary Magdalene Church in 2023.

George enjoyed a slice of pizza while attending a London cricket match with his dad in July 2023.

Louis, George and Charlotte rode off to London's Westminster Abbey for their grandfather King Charles III's coronation ceremony in May 2023—though the monarch immediately acceded to the throne following the death of his mother Queen Elizabeth II in September 2022.

Charlotte gave her dad a thumbs-up as they attended the Commonwealth Games' swimming event with Kate in August 2022.

He also joined his older siblings in a carriage ride to mark the momentous occasion.

As William, Kate, George and Charlotte headed to 2022's Easter service at St. George's Chapel in Windsor, fans might have noticed some bunny was missing. However, the service may have been too long for Louis, who was only 4 years old at the time and already proving he could get into some adorable mischief.

MCU Surprise: ‘VisionQuest’ Finale Teases the Return of [SPOILER] in ‘Avengers: Doomsday’

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

SPOILER WARNING: This story contains major spoilers for “Forever,” the finale episode of “VisionQuest,” now streaming on Disney+.

The WandaVision Saga has come to a close with the final hour of the Disney+ series “VisionQuest.” But it seems as though the story of the ultrapowerful Scarlet Witch, the synthezoid Vision and their superpowered children will continue in the MCU.

The finale episode sees Paul Bettany’s Vision reunite with his and Wanda Maximoff’s (Elizabeth Olsen) grown-up sons, Ruaridh Mollica’s Tommy Maximoff (who was key to the series) and Joe Locke’s Billy Maximoff (whose eleventh-hour arrival completes the family reunion). Then, the post-credit scene replays the end of 2022’s feature film “Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness,” where Wanda eradicated the Darkhold from all of the multiverse before (seemingly) being crushed from within a mountain. Related Stories Amy Poehler Tells Noah Wyle ‘The Pitt’ Needs More Romance: If You’re ‘Gonna Show the Guts, You Gotta Show Some Butts’ 'The Pitt' and 'ER' Are Similar Enough to Allow Crichton Suit, Appeals Court Rules

However, this time, the scene continues with the appearance of Robert Downey Jr.’s Doctor Doom, who lifts the rubble off Wanda and addresses her.

“Scarlett Witch, awaken — your power will be mine,” Doom says, as Wanda gasps and opens her eyes.

The coda confirms fan speculation that Olsen’s Wanda would return as the Avengers face off against Doom in future Marvel projects, which could include “Avengers: Doomsday,” out Dec. 18.

Ahead of the finale, Marvel tweeted a message written in binary code, which fans were quick to decode as a quote from “WandaVision.” Though the tweet was written with a series of 0s and 1s, it translated to: “We could never truly leave each other, even if we tried.” (The line is from the show’s 2021 finale.)

Olsen first appeared as Wanda in the post-credits scene for 2014’s “Captain America: The Winter Soldier,” which set up her full-fledged debut in 2015’s “Avengers: Age of Ultron.” Despite her wide-ranging powers of telekinesis and mind manipulation, Wanda’s first forays in the MCU were laden with tragedy, including the death of her brother Pietro (Aaron Taylor-Johnson), the devastation of her home country of Sokovia in “Age of Ultron,” and then the death of her beloved Vision (Paul Bettany) in 2018’s “Avengers: Infinity War.”

That trauma led to 2021’s “WandaVision,” Marvel Studios‘ first TV series for Disney+, in which Wanda’s overwhelming grief led her to take over an entire New Jersey town and transform it into a recreation of American sitcoms — complete with a reconstituted Vision and two twin sons, Billy and Tommy. By the end of the series, she had to let go of Vision and her sons, all while her interactions with the witch Agatha Harkness (Kathryn Hahn) revealed Wanda to be the Scarlet Witch, one of the most powerful beings in the Marvel universe. Agatha’s witchy, post-“WandaVision” escapades continued in the 2024 Disney+ series “Agatha All Along,” which introduced Joe Locke as Wanda and Vision’s grown-up son Billy Maximoff.

Wanda’s story continued in 2022’s feature film “Doctor Strange in the Multiverse of Madness,” in which her grief from the loss of her children led her to turn fully evil as the film’s central villain. She brutally killed anyone standing in opposition to her desire to find her boys within the multiverse, fueled by the corrupting book of magic, the Darkhold. By the end, Wanda understood all the harm she had caused, and she eradicated the Darkhold from all of the multiverse before (seemingly) being crushed from within Wundagore mountain.

When asked by Variety in 2022 whether she hoped to play Wanda again, however, Olsen sounded optimistic.

“It’s weird that I’m expecting to return, but no one’s told me I’m doing anything!” she said with a laugh. “But in my mind, I’m just making the assumption that they’ll have me again. I don’t know to what capacity, but I hope I’m back. I hope there’s also more fun to be had in something different.”

US House blocks bid to impeach Defence Secretary Pete Hegseth, for now

📅 Nov 11, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Watch: Republicans vote to block Hegseth impeachment from reaching full vote

An effort to impeach US Defence Secretary Pete Hegseth, accusing him of waging war on Iran without congressional authorisation, has failed on Capitol Hill.

A majority of the House of Representatives agreed to refer impeachment articles to a committee - essentially killing the effort introduced by dissident Republican congressman Thomas Massie of Kentucky - for now.

In his first news briefing from the Pentagon in six months, Hegseth called the impeachment effort a "farce" and "sabotage".

The bid to find the cabinet secretary guilty of misconduct and ultimately remove him from office was seen as a long shot as it could have required a number of other Republicans to defy President Donald Trump.

Massie was the only Republican to vote against moving the resolution to the Republican-led House Judiciary Committee on Tuesday.

His effort failed in a 211-204 vote, with more than a dozen members, including 11 Democrats, missing the vote.

The move to send the measure to the panel effectively kills the effort, as there is only a short period of time left in this Congress before Democrats take control in January.

President Trump celebrated the vote in a post on Truth Social, saying the effort "has miserably FAILED".

"Again, Victories in Venezuela and Iran, the Best Recruitment Numbers in the History of the Military (Long waiting lists to get in!), and Spirit like rarely seen before, led to the EXONERATION of a Great American Patriot," Trump said, referring to Hegseth.

After the vote, Massie said he "wasn't surprised" but "was disappointed", according to BBC's US media partner CBS.

"My diagnosis of Congress is these people know what to do," he added. "They just can't bring themselves to do it, even when it would benefit them politically."

Massie had previously acknowledged his impeachment bid may not succeed immediately, but argued it could help push Trump to replace Hegseth before Democrats can launch their own impeachment process.

Hegseth: Impeachment effort is "farce" and "stunt"

The Kentucky congressman also said that removing Hegseth now would give the Senate time to approve a replacement while Republicans were still in power.

Impeachment resolutions can be carried past the end of a congressional term, meaning Democrats would be able to take it up next year.

The Democrats will take control of both chambers of Congress in January following a successful midterm election that allowed them control of both chambers.

House Speaker Mike Johnson, a Republican, had predicted earlier in the day that the impeachment bid would fail.

In the eight articles of impeachment formally introduced in the House on Monday, Massie said Hegseth was "breaking the law and must be held accountable" for fighting a war in Iran for more than 90 days without lawmakers' approval.

The impeachment articles also accused Hegseth of overseeing "extrajudicial killings" in the US campaign against boats alleged to be carrying drugs in the Pacific Ocean and Caribbean Sea.

At a news conference on Tuesday, Hegseth called efforts to impeach him a political stunt by a Trump administration opponent who was "disgruntled and defeated".

Hegseth also argued Massie's resolution endangered national security, because it communicated to US adversaries "that the United States will actually punish its own warriors and its own civilian leadership for successfully executing lawful military operations".

"This is not patriotism. This is sabotage," he said.

He echoed some statements Trump had made during a surprise appearance at the White House briefing room on Monday, including touting a rise in military recruitment levels.

But Hegseth said Massie was motivated by policy differences and "not high crimes, not misdemeanours, not treason, not bribery, not the kinds of offences the founders intended when they wrote the impeachment clause".

Massie, who lost re-election earlier this year to a Trump-backed opponent, has been critical of Trump and the war in Iran since it began in February.

He had already incurred the president's ire for demanding the Trump administration release files on deceased sex offender Jeffrey Epstein.

While Hegseth has survived politically for now, many lawmakers - including some Republicans - have questioned how long he can last in the administration.

Texas Senator Ted Cruz, a Republican, said on Tuesday he expected Hegseth to resign or be fired by Trump.

The 10 Most Hopeless Movies of the 21st Century, Ranked

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

There are some movies that leave you feeling uplifted, inspired, or even ready to take on the world. Sadly, these aren't those movies. These are the most hopeless ones of the 21st century that take a much bleaker view of the human experience, whether that be exploring grief, addiction, cruelty, loneliness, or the terrifying possibility that some situations simply can't be fixed.

From the backdrop of a dying planet to stories that focus on ordinary people watching society completely collapse, these movies aren't interested in reassuring happy endings. They're often difficult to sit through, all while attaining their unforgettable emotional impact. So buckle up, because these are some real heavy ones.

Set in a dystopian future where Earth has been rendered uninhabitable by environmental destruction, a group of passengers boards a luxury spaceship for a routine voyage to Mars. Unfortunately, the ship soon collides with space debris, leaving it knocked permanently off course, without a way to correct its trajectory. Now, drifting further into the vastness of space, the passengers are forced to face the terrifying possibility that they may never reach their final destination.

Aniara is a chilling mediation on mortality, denial, and the human need to believe there's something waiting for us at the end of the journey. For one, throughout this forgotten sci-fi flick, the survivors initially cling to distractions, relationships, and artificial comforts—only for those to eventually become increasingly meaningless as the reality of their circumstances set in. And by the time its final moments arrive, the film has made the sheer indifference of the universe feel almost unbearable.

After receiving a prestigious grant, theater director Caden Cotard (Philip Seymour Hoffman) begins an ambitious stage production that recreates an entire city inside a warehouse. But as the project grows increasingly elaborate, the boundary between production and reality begins to disappear, all while Caden becomes consumed by his own existential anxieties and relationships.

Few films capture the exhausting weight of simply being alive quite like Synecdoche, New York. Its surreal structure reflects Caden's inability to make sense of his existence, as time slips away and his grand artistic undertaking becomes an increasingly futile attempt to understand himself, those around him, and mortality itself. Funny in places, with an overwhelming sadness beneath the absurdity, this unsettling portrait of a struggling man is a stellar directorial debut by the visionary Charlie Kaufman.

When hunter Llewelyn Moss (Josh Brolin) stumbles upon the aftermath of a drug deal gone wrong, he makes the fateful decision to take a case filled with millions of dollars. Unfortunately, that puts him in the crosshairs of Anton Chigurh (Javier Bardem), a ruthless hitman who will stop at almost nothing to retrieve the money. But as Sheriff Ed Tom Bell (Tommy Lee Jones) tries to track them both down, he finds himself confronting a level of violence and cruelty that seems increasingly beyond his understanding.

The Coen brothers' adaptation of No Country for Old Men is a masterclass in tension, but its bleakness comes from something more unsettling than its brutal violence. Chigurh feels less like a conventional villain than an unstoppable force of chaos, while Bell's growing disillusionment reflects a world that no longer seems to operate by any moral code he recognizes. There are no easy victories or comforting explanations here, just people trying to make sense of a cruel and indifferent world.

Four films, four completely different ways of handling money, trouble and other people. This is the one you would survive, why it suits you, and what it would cost you to be there.

You have quietly arranged your life so that very little can get to you, and a surprising number of people find that infuriating. You would be completely at home here.

You talk your way through life and it works far more often than it should. Give you a bad situation and an audience and you will find a way out of both at once.

You would survive Fargo because you are the only kind of person who does: patient, decent, and completely unimpressed by anybody trying to be clever.

You do not expect the world to be fair and you stopped being surprised about it a long time ago. That clear-eyed quality is exactly what this film asks for.

With civilization collapsed and most of the world's survivors reduced to desperate scavengers, an unnamed father (Viggo Mortensen) and his young son (Kodi Smit-McPhee) travel in search of warmer weather and a semblance of safety. But their journey is a constant struggle as they scramble for food, avoid hostile strangers, and hold on to the small sense of humanity that separates them from the surrounding horrors.

What makes The Road so crushing is that its bleak world is never just a backdrop for survival thrills. Yes, the father's determination to protect his son is deeply moving, but it also comes with the terrible knowledge that he may not be able to keep him safe forever. Their bond offers the film its only real source of warmth, making every encounter with the cruelty of the outside world feel that much more devastating. It's a story about love enduring in the worst possible circumstances, but one that never pretends love is enough to fix everything.

Béla Tarr's The Turin Horse begins with a mysterious incident involving a famous philosopher, who is said to have broken down after witnessing a horse being beaten in the street. From there, the film follows the horse's owner, Ohlsdorfer (János Derzsi) and his daughter (Erika Bók), as they go about their daily lives on an isolated farm. But when the horse suddenly refuses to work, increasingly strange events suggest that even their small, familiar world is beginning to come apart.

Shot in long, hypnotic takes and told through a deliberately repetitive rhythm, The Turin Horse makes the slow collapse of existence feel almost physical. Meals become a mechanical ritual, the weather grows more hostile, and the characters' options seem to shrink with every passing day. There's no dramatic catastrophe or last-minute revelation to offer relief. Instead, Tarr builds an atmosphere of total exhaustion, as though the world is simply running out of reasons to continue.

After the death of his brother, Lee Chandler (Casey Affleck) is forced to return to his hometown, where he discovers that he has been named guardian of his teenage nephew, Patrick (Lucas Hedges). Unfortunately, the arrangement means putting his solitary life in Boston on hold and confronting a community filled with painful memories.

Manchester by the Sea is heartbreaking because it understands that grief doesn't always lead to healing, closure, or some grand emotional breakthrough. Lee's past has left him carrying a guilt so profound that even the possibility of a fresh start feels impossible, and Affleck's performance makes that pain all the more affecting. Not every wound can be fixed, and the film is simply brave enough to leave that truth unresolved.

Lars von Trier's psychological drama follows two sisters, Justine (Kirsten Dunst) and Claire (Charlotte Gainsbourg), as a mysterious planet approaches Earth. The film is split into two parts: first Justine's lavish wedding reception, which descends into utter chaos, then, the days leading up to the planet's predicted collision, when the sisters react to the impending apocalypse in very different ways.

Rather than building towards a frantic race for survival, von Trier uses the looming catastrophe to explore depression, anxiety, and the strange comfort that can come from knowing there's nothing left to fight against. Dunst gives a remarkable performance, capturing Justine's despair without reducing her to it, while the film's haunting imagery makes its cosmic destruction feel undoubtedly terrifying. Its hopelessness is total, and yet there's an eerie serenity in the way it embraces the inevitable.

Selma Ježková (Björk) is a Czech immigrant working in a rural American factory, who is gradually losing her eyesight due to a hereditary condition. Determined to prevent her young son from suffering the same fate, she works tirelessly to save enough money for an operation—occasionally escaping the hardships of her daily life by retreating into elaborate musical fantasies. Sadly, a devastating betrayal threatens everything she's worked towards.

The contrast between the film's whimsical sequences and its increasingly brutal reality makes Dancer in the Dark almost unbearably sad. Selma's optimism and selflessness are admirable, but they're also exploited by the people around her, so much so that her maternal determination becomes the very thing that destroys her. Björk delivers an extraordinary performance, bringing a fragile, almost childlike sincerity to a character whose kindness is met with astonishing cruelty. And by the time the film reaches its final act, its refusal to offer an easy comfort feels downright merciless.

Oh Dae-su (Choi Min-sik) is a man who's mysteriously imprisoned in a private cell for 15 years without being told why. And when he's suddenly released, he sets out to discover who abducted him and what motivated his captivity—only to find himself drawn into an increasingly elaborate game orchestrated by a strange man.

Oldboy may be famous for its ferocious action, but its true horror lies in the devastating revelations waiting at the end of Dae-su's journey. Park Chan-wook turns a seemingly straightforward revenge story into a vicious examination of guilt, obsession, and the destructive power of secrets—with Choi anchoring this film with a masterful performance. Better still, the final twist doesn't simply shock. It leaves the audience questioning whether survival can even be considered a victory.

Darren Aranofsky's psychological drama follows four people whose lives are gradually consumed by addiction. Harry Goldfarb (Jared Leto) and his girlfriend Marion (Jennifer Connelly) dream of building a better future together, while his friend Tyrone (Marlon Wayans) hopes to escape the poverty and instability of his upbringing. Meanwhile, Harry's mother, Sara (Ellen Burstyn), becomes obsessed with losing weight after receiving the prospect of appearing on television, even as that turns her to prescription pills.

Few films are as relentlessly bleak as Requiem for a Dream, which charts the slow destruction of its characters with an intensity that's difficult to shake. The frantic editing, distorted visuals, and haunting score make their spirals feel almost suffocating, with stellar performances (like Burstyn's) amplifying the devastation. There's no romanticizing addiction here, nor any suggestion that good intentions can protect people from its consequences. By the end, all four are left with utter wreckage, making this one of the most harrowing films of the century.

Johnny Depp’s ‘Ebenezer’ Officially Earns Glowing First Reactions as Critics Hail His Hollywood Return

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Following his high-profile legal battle with his ex, Amber Heard, which dominated headlines and altered the public perception of both, Johnny Depp wisely took time away from the spotlight, having now been away from a major Hollywood production since 2018's Fantastic Beasts: The Crimes of Grindelwald. However, this month, the man behind Captain Jack Sparrow is back, headlining Ti West’s Ebenezer, the latest project based on Charles Dickens' iconic 1843 novella.

Looking to add the iconic Ebenezer Scrooge to his pantheon of memorable characters, Depp already made a surprise appearance in character during Collider's Directors on Directing Panel at San Diego Comic-Con, setting the wheels in motion for his potentially glorious comeback to the Hollywood spotlight. Ahead of the film's scheduled release by Paramount Pictures on November 13, 2026, the first response from critics is officially in, but what exactly are they saying about Depp's return?

"Johnny Depp is magnetic throughout Scrooge’s soft redemption arc leading to a finale that delivers more emotional resonance than its predecessors," wrote UK Film Critic Phil Roberts, who was one of many impressed by Depp's typically energetic performance. Ebenezer is a "wickedly fun holiday treat and strong comeback vehicle for its star," according to FilmSpeak’s chief film critic, Tom Chatalbash, adding that "Depp is a fiendish delight as Scrooge."

Not every critic was blown away by Depp's comeback. Critic Ren Vieira said that "nothing’s distinct or unique about this version," adding, "it’s just Depp doing cooky Scrooge, nothing more than his old tricks." Critic and writer MaryAnn Johanson said the film was "Utterly superfluous," with Robert Ortiz saying, "the film commits 2 sins of being forgettable and just boring, especially if you’ve seen a Christmas carol before."

Following the release of Ebenezer, the Depp comeback train is only just starting its journey. On March 26, 2027, Depp will star in Day Drinker, an action thriller directed by Marc Webb, who will be hoping for his own comeback after helming the disastrous live-action Snow White remake in 2025. Depp will star alongside Madelyn Cline (Outer Banks), upcoming Oscars hopeful Penélope Cruz (Pirates of the Caribbean: On Stranger Tides), and Manu Ríos (Elite) in Day Drinker.

A woman was shot during the dispute and the suspect is still at large, police said.
