73 Years Later, John Wayne's Greatest Final Quote From 'Hondo' Still Officially Defines the Western Genre

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

What defines a genre? Is it a set of pre-established or agreed upon conventions? Is it a specific aesthetic or sort of character followed? When it comes to the Western, the genre is recognizable by those who know it best—and sometimes, that includes the characters within it. If there’s a single concluding line spoken by John Wayne in his big-screen horse operas that most perfectly defines the genre, it’s undoubtedly Hondo Lane's final remarks about the Apache in Hondo. In just a few short words, the hero makes it clear what this genre is truly all about.

As the titular hero watches the Apache take off in retreat, hoping to regroup before their next conflict with the U.S. Cavalry, Buffalo Baker (underrated Hollywood talent Ward Bond) notes that this will be the end of the tribe. “Yeah. End of a way of life. Too bad, it's a good way,” Hondo replies before urging the wagons forward. It’s a small moment of pause and reflection that, on the surface, appears to be nothing more than Hondo acknowledging the fading dominion of the Apache. However, upon further examination, we can see that it’s a line that speaks rivers of truth into the genre itself. The Western is largely about a period in American history that bridges the gap between the untamed West and the civilization that followed. Countless Western protagonists, Hondo included, are caught between the gun-toting life they’ve always known and the coming progress that threatens to strip down that identity for the sake of a more "tamed" society.

Many a Western teases the “end of the Wild West.” It's a common theme in the genre that reaches from some of the Duke's earliest pictures to his last. In this regard, perhaps Hondo is no different. The Western hero, unless domesticated, has no place in this new, "civilized" world. This line uttered by the Duke reflects the constant struggle that genre heroes—including folks like Alan Ladd’s Shane, Paul Newman and Robert Redford’s Butch and Sundance, or even Clint Eastwood’s “Man With No Name”—endure with each new interpretation. Hondo may be one of the best examples of the basic horse opera plot—one based on a short story by famed Western author Louis L'Amour—but it concludes with a sober-minded reflection that speaks to the Western genre as a whole, no matter what form it takes. (Consider reading the novelization, by the way, which L'Amour penned himself and can easily be finished in a weekend!)

The Western "way" of living was always best embodied on the big screen by John Wayne. Hondo is just one of many examples of Duke-led horse operas that could easily define the genre. The Man Who Shot Liberty Valance, Stagecoach, The Cowboys, Rio Bravo, True Grit, and, of course, The Searchers are the most obvious films that come to mind. But none of them conclude with these simple words that remind the viewer just what the genre is about — and just like the Wild West era of history came to an end, so too does the Western film continue to remind us that this brief window into American history is a limited period, and the Western heroes are no more. Perhaps this side of Wayne's decades-long career, we can best understand that idea. After all, the Western genre is not at the same height that it was when it dominated film, television, and radio in postwar America.

Perhaps there's something to the fact that the genre's golden age concluded in the mid-1970s, around the same time John Wayne himself rounded out his career. In 1975, Gunsmoke, the long-running television series that Wayne helped launch in 1955, came to an abrupt end the same way the Wild West era did itself. A year later, the Duke's final on-screen role as J.B. Books in The Shootist premiered in theaters. This was a far cry from Hondo, but there's no doubt that, by looking at the past, the 1953 picture was able to predict the Western's future. Of course, the Western itself has never completely died. Its popularity may have waned over the years, but the horse opera continues to captivate viewers today for the same reason it did back when Hondo hit theaters — folks are looking back at a time long past, hoping it will reveal something more about our future.

Hondo is available for streaming on Prime Video.

Huupe Mini 2 Review: More Game Than It Can Handle

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If your mom was one of those who always said, “Don’t play ball in the house,” well, she’s going to absolutely hate the Huupe Mini 2.

Huupe manufactures smart basketball hoops and add-on kits for existing setups that track shots, light up the board when a point is registered, and include social features like global challenges and leaderboards. The Huupe Mini 2 is the pint-sized, indoor version of that idea, clipping over the top of a door so you can shoot hoops without ever having to leave the room.

I’ve been testing one out for weeks, and while it’s a fun diversion, some of its features overreach beyond its core functionality, resulting in an experience that bricks as much as it dunks.

The Huupe Mini 2 is, effectively, a smart television with a basketball hoop mounted to it. Or rather, a smart TV that comes with a basketball hoop that you have to mount yourself, courtesy of four screws and a little elbow grease. The LCD screen measures 19 inches diagonally, plus a hefty border that includes stereo speakers and a camera used to track you and your b-ball prowess. (Total size of the backboard is 25 inches diagonally, which fits comfortably within a door frame.)

A 5-inch inflatable rubber basketball is included, along with a small hand pump and needle. Also in the box are a small but otherwise traditional remote control—required to do anything with the Huupe—and a USB-C charger and lengthy cable so you can top up the battery without removing it from the door.

After a quick charge, the unit is ready to set up. I was surprised to discover there’s no app for the device; while larger Huupe hardware works with one, the Mini 2 is completely self-contained and web-based. Just connect the backboard to your Wi-Fi network through a process that you step through with the remote, just like you would on any modern television.

What’s new vs. the original Huupe Mini? According to the company, most of the upgrades are behind the scenes: a faster CPU, a larger battery (rated lifespan is now four hours), better display quality, and “significantly” better shot tracking. New games are also included, which I’ll cover in the sections to come.

Once connected to the web, the Huupe Mini 2 opens up a world of entertainment to you, and, to my great surprise, not all of it is basketball-related. At first blush, the options available are actually quite daunting. It’s all arranged into traditional tiles on the home screen, which you scroll through with the remote.

Just Huupe is the board’s most basic scoring system, tracking shots and recording your shooting percentage in an untimed session. If you want to get competitive, Huupe Live and the Daily Prize Challenge are social games where you challenge players worldwide to score the most points in a given time frame. The winner gets a nominal prize—10 bucks for the daily challenge, a “cozy sweatsuit” for a monthlong challenge. Other games are fairly self-explanatory, like Most 3 Pointers in 90 Seconds and Family Mode (basically Horse).

Many of the games require online friends to play against; if you don’t have pals with their own Huupe Minis, you’ll probably be locked out of these modes—not that they add much beyond their multiplayer features.

Things get a little kookier—and buggier—from there. For example, Huupe Flyers is designed to be played without a ball. It’s a gesture-based version of Flappy Bird, wherein you literally flap your arms around to keep a basketball in the air, tracking your movements via the camera much like an Xbox Kinect. Unfortunately, unlike the ball-based games, I was never able to play it, as it failed the calibration process every time I tried to fire it up.

The system also works as a fairly straightforward streaming platform. Included are apps for YouTube, HBO Max, ESPN, Disney+, Prime Video, Hulu, Spotify, and more. There’s one glaring omission in that list, of course, and I’m not talking about Paramount+ but rather Netflix. Without Netflix support, can you even call yourself a streaming device? It’s also worth noting that the built-in speakers are surprisingly weak. On some apps the audio is OK; on others it’s nearly inaudible.

The Huupe Mini 2 includes not just a sensor that can detect when a basket is successfully made; it also includes a camera to track how far the shooter is from the backboard. This is used to assign point values to various shots (depending on the specific game you’re playing), with more points earned the farther away you are from the hoop.

The good news is that in weeks of playing on the Huupe Mini 2, I never noticed a time when a successful basket was recorded as a miss or vice versa. The distance calculation, however, is another thing. I’ve shot from across the room and had it count as 1 point, while some shots from just a few feet away ring up as 3 points. I could never figure out much rhyme or reason for this variability, except that shots straight-on to the backboard generally registered as lower point values, even if they were distant, while shots from the wings earned more. Of course, navigating all of this is highly dependent on the geometry of your room. Unless you have the Huupe Mini 2 set up in an empty garage, you are probably, like me, going to have plenty of obstacles that get in the way of a lot of your shots—and plenty of crawling under furniture to retrieve wayward balls.

I can say with authority that it is harder to make a basket on the Huupe Mini 2 than on a regulation basketball goal, even at a very close distance. The hoop is small, and the ball tends to rebound wildly off the springy backboard. It’s quite unforgiving, and it didn’t really make me want to spend a lot of time playing, chasing balls that bounced into the corner or rolled under the bed.

The other big problem: When the included inflatable ball hits the Huupe Mini 2, the impact is extremely loud, to the point where I was promptly informed by my wife that it was not appropriate for use when anyone else was in the house. While Huupe says the device is intended solely for use with the included ball, I found a workaround by purchasing a 5-inch foam basketball from Amazon, which makes a bit less noise on impact and also softens the rebound a bit. It has had zero trouble registering baskets.

Battery life is another issue. While four hours is plenty of playing time, the battery continues to drain when the Huupe is in standby mode, which meant I would play a few games in the morning, ostensibly shut the Huupe off, then come back in the afternoon to a completely dead device. The Huupe can run with the power cord attached, but only once the battery has charged to at least about 5 percent.

Unfortunately, even turning the power off is puzzlingly difficult. The power button on my remote control did nothing; I ultimately had to turn the system on and off with the physical button on the bottom of the backboard.

At $429 (on deep discount from a hefty list price of $799), the Huupe Mini 2 sits perhaps just over the edge of what might pass for a quirky holiday gift—and I think it’s safe to say no one is buying this device for themselves. You’d have to be a huge basketball nut to get enough mileage out of the device to make it worthwhile. I expect you would also have to loathe leaving your room. With today’s kids, who knows.

James Cook's 31 carries vs. Vikings were the most in any NFL game this season

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Against a Vikings defense that has been teeing off on opposing quarterbacks this season, Bills coach Joe Brady knew he needed to lean on his running back on Monday night.

And so he did, with Bills running back James Cook getting 31 carries for 107 yards and a touchdown in a 23-6 win over the Vikings.

Cook’s 31 carries were the most any running back has had in any NFL game this season.

Cook led the NFL with 1,621 rushing yards last season, but he’s actually been even more productive so far this season. He’s averaging both more carries per game and more yards per game in 2026 than he did in 2025. Josh Allen makes the Bills’ offense go, but the Bills rely on their running back as much as any team in the NFL, and on Monday night, Cook delivered.

Grey’s Anatomy Season 23 Is Officially One of the Shortest Seasons Ever

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

If there's one thing Grey's Anatomy fans have learned over the past 21 years, it's that you can never really have too much time at Grey Sloan Memorial Hospital. Between the medical emergencies, complicated romances, and the seemingly endless collection of disasters that somehow manage to find their way into Seattle, the ABC drama has rarely struggled to fill a season. In fact, some of its earlier installments stretched beyond 20 episodes, giving audiences months of weekly chaos.

Unfortunately, anyone hoping for another lengthy season this year is going to be disappointed, because one of the show's longest-serving stars has finally confirmed what fans were worried about. Chandra Wilson, who has portrayed Miranda Bailey since the show's 2005 debut, has confirmed on the Chicks in the Office podcast that Grey's Anatomy Season 23 currently consists of just 13 episodes, making it the third-shortest season in the series' history.

During the podcast appearance, Wilson explained that the show's upcoming 500th episode has become a major talking point among the cast and crew. And while the current episode order might make that milestone seem a little further away, she has a plan for getting there.

Yeah, we talk about 500. 500 is a really good number, and I had, like, kind of worked out how we were going to do it. I said, so we’re doing 13 this season. If we implode that and actually do a 13 and a 14 in a 2-hour season finale, that’ll put us at 480 at the end, right? So, now we only need 20, and we could do, like, a full 20-episode season next season instead of 13, and that would be 500 at the end or do 19 and start season 25 with [the 500th].

The medical drama stars Ellen Pompeo (Old School) as Meredith Grey, Chandra Wilson (Philadelphia) as Miranda Bailey, James Pickens Jr. (The X-Files) as Richard Webber, Kevin McKidd (Rome) as Owen Hunt, Kim Raver (24) as Teddy Altman, Caterina Scorsone (Private Practice) as Amelia Shepherd, Camilla Luddington (True Blood) as Jo Wilson, Anthony Hill (Watchmen) as Winston Ndugu, and Adelaide Kane (Reign) as Jules Millin.

Created by Shonda Rhimes, Grey's Anatomy first premiered in March 2005 and has since become one of the longest-running scripted primetime dramas in American television history. Grey's Anatomy Season 23 airs Thursdays at 10 p.m. ET on ABC, with episodes streaming the following day on Hulu. The season currently has an order of 13 episodes.

They seek comfort from one another, and, at times, more than just friendship. Together they discover that neither medicine nor relationships can be defined in black and white.

Russell Brand’s Ex-Girlfriend Accuses Him of “Violence, Coercive Control and Abuse”

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

TV producer Jemima Goldsmith took to social media after Brand received a 10-year prison sentence for rape and sexual assault: "This was a difficult period in my life which has had a lasting impact."

In the wake of his rape conviction, Russell Brand‘s ex-girlfriend has accused him of “coercive control and abuse” while they were together.

The disgraced presenter and comedian was sentenced to 10 years and six months in prison on Monday after being found guilty on one count of rape and three counts of sexual assault by a London jury.

The court heard harrowing witness impact statements before the sentencing. One victim said: “I’ve been in shock and turmoil for weeks. I was extremely upset and traumatised. I didn’t have any respect for myself… I was thinking I was damaged goods — scared of giving off signals that could be taken the wrong way.” She continued that there were many times she felt “physically sick” seeing Brand in the media. “I just want to say, a leopard may change its spots, but it’s still a leopard.” Related Stories Movies 'Summer School, 2001,' the First Czech-Vietnamese Feature, Is Coming to America via Cinemas, Amazon (Exclusive) Movies Claes Bang, Valerie Pachner Drama 'I Is Another' Sells Wide Ahead of AFM

The highly publicized trial and conviction have raised substantial questions across the industry over how Brand went unreported for so long. Just last week, a former associate of Brand’s accused of watching and laughing while the entertainer sexually assaulted a woman was asked to “step back” from his producing role. Jack Bayles, the head of comedy at independent TV production company Various Artists Limited (VAL), has worked on Michaela Coel’s I May Destroy You and Kat Sadler’s Such Brave Girls.

Now, TV producer Jemima Goldsmith, the ex-wife of former Pakistani prime minister and cricketer Imran Khan and the daughter of billionaire entrepreneur James Goldsmith, has taken to social media about her relationship with Brand. She said she was forced “to call the police to have him removed from my house in 2014 during a violent incident,” but did not end up pressing charges.

Goldsmith, who dated Brand for around a year in 2014, said the decision not to press charges or go public was down to her being worried about the impact of press attention on her then-teenage children. “I have neither seen nor spoken to Brand since that period in 2014,” she wrote in a widely shared post on X. “Whilst my pain in relation to this pales in comparison to what the women have testified in court have suffered, this was a difficult period in my life which has had a lasting impact.”

I am going to describe some painful and personal things in relation to Russell Brand because I have been put in a position now where I feel that I have no choice other than to do so, given the misleading reports and speculation around the facts of a harassment injunction that was…

She says she “assisted” the journalists who sought to expose Brand’s behavior and was willing to “testify in court to his abusiveness” if those newspapers were then sued. “I have spent much of my professional career trying to support unfairly demonised and silenced women,” Goldsmith wrote.

The post also addresses recent reports about a woman named Szilvia Berki, who in October 2015 is said to have received an injunction from Brand and Goldsmith that banned Berki from harassing them. Berki had also accused Brand of assault at Goldsmith’s home around that time.

“The commentary and media reporting this week has presented an untrue narrative, which has opened me up to vile hatred and abuse online,” Goldsmith wrote. She explained that Berki was at Goldsmith’s house on June 7, 2014 to give Brand a massage. “By the time she arrived, [Brand] had decided he didn’t want a massage and asked her to leave. She was clearly offended. I was present the whole time he was with her, and I did not see anything untoward, despite the fact that he was later found guilty of sexually assaulting other women and he later physically assaulted me,” she continued.

“If I saw any man abuse any woman under any circumstances, I would do my utmost to stop them, without hesitation. Ms Berki later reported to the police that Brand had pushed her in the back whilst she was leaving the house and that she also said she was ‘attacked’ by my dog, which she described in her police statement as my dog licking her face.” The police investigated Berki’s complaint, and Goldsmith said she cooperated fully by providing CCTV footage.

“Following an investigation into Ms Berki’s allegations, police concluded that there was no case to answer and that no criminal offence had been committed,” said Goldsmith. But Berki began selling untrue stories to the tabloids, Goldsmith said, and began a campaign of “vicious antisemitic and threatening abuse online and in emails” toward her.

The post ended with Goldsmith saying she hopes the information will “clarify speculation.” She said: “I am glad and relieved that Russell Brand has finally faced justice.”

‘NCIS’ Still Hasn’t Found the Perfect Replacement for Its Most Important Character

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Amanda M. Castro is a Network TV writer at Collider and a New York–based journalist whose work has appeared in Newsweek, where she is an Associate Editor, and The U.S. Sun, where she previously served as a Senior Consumer Reporter.

She specializes in network television coverage, delivering sharp, thoughtful analysis of long-running procedural hits and ambitious new dramas across broadcast TV. At Collider, Amanda explores character arcs, storytelling trends, and the cultural impact of network series that keep audiences tuning in week after week.

Born and raised in Puerto Rico, Amanda is bilingual and holds a degree in Communication, Film, and Media Studies from the University of New Haven.

NCIS has been bringing in new agents for quite some time, yet it still hasn't filled the gap left by a certain character. Cote de Pablo’s contribution as Ziva David dates back to 2013, but her departure has affected the characters around her. Even now, with Michael Weatherly back as Tony DiNozzo in Season 24, the series is finding reasons to bring her up, which is a pretty remarkable achievement for a character who hasn't been a regular cast member in more than a decade.

Ziva has had an unusual introduction to team NCIS. She arrived in the Season 3 premiere, "Kill Ari (Part 1)," after Kate Todd's (Sasha Alexander) death, and she happened to be the half-sister of the man responsible. Ari Haswari (Rudolf Martin) had murdered Kate, and Ziva was the one who ultimately shot him to save Gibbs (Mark Harmon). Understandably, nobody was thrilled to have her around at first. She had to convince an office full of suspicious colleagues that she belonged there, and the show gave her plenty of room to do exactly that.

Plenty of memorable agents have come after Ziva, and they don't need to be carbon copies of her to be worthwhile, but her departure left a specific gap for a reason. Her story connected the procedural's action to its family drama, while her relationships with Gibbs and Tony gave the series two very different emotional threads to follow.

When Ziva joined NCIS, she had already experienced more than most of her fellow employees could imagine. Her father, Eli David (Michael Nouri), headed the Mossad, and she grew up where discipline and responsibility took priority over everything else. Ziva served in the Israeli army before being recruited into the Mossad, lost her youngest sister, Tali, to an explosion, and learned how to survive in a world that demanded she be cruel.

From the start, Ziva clearly had the skills to succeed. She spoke several languages, was skilled at hand-to-hand combat, and performed well even in the most difficult situations, but what made her interesting was that she spent so many years learning to accept violence as a solution to everything that she had to learn how to live without it.

The change became especially important after her capture and torture in Somalia. Gibbs, Tony, and McGee (Sean Murray) helped bring her home in the Season 7 premiere, "Truth or Consequences," but getting her back was only part of the story. Ziva eventually resigned from Mossad and pursued a position at NCIS, choosing to build a life with people who had become her family.

She also had a personality that was entirely her own. Her difficulty with American idioms became a recurring source of comedy, particularly when Tony was around to make things worse. Ziva could be intimidating one minute and completely baffled by an expression the next. De Pablo understood that the humor made her feel like someone you actually wanted to spend time with.

The relationship between Gibbs and Ziva is one of the best examples of how much her character changed after joining NCIS. Their introduction wasn't exactly warm; Gibbs had every reason to question her loyalties, and Ziva wasn't particularly accustomed to explaining herself to anyone. Still, the circumstances surrounding Ari's death gave them a connection that neither could easily dismiss.

One of their earliest defining moments comes in "Kill Ari (Part 2)." After Ziva saves Gibbs by killing her own brother, she visits him in the hospital. When he struggles to remember what happened, she takes his hand and slaps herself with it, prompting him to remember that she saved his life. The moment ends with Ziva breaking down in his arms. It's a rare glimpse of how much the experience has affected her, especially since she's spent so much time trying to appear unaffected.

Their relationship continues to deepen in Season 7's "Good Cop, Bad Cop." After Ziva is cleared in an investigation involving the deaths aboard the Damocles, Gibbs kisses her on the forehead. It is a small gesture, but it matters to anyone with issues around her father. Eventually, Gibbs becomes the person Ziva is comfortable enough to show her vulnerability around, which is something she lacked growing up – unconditional acceptance and belonging.

This will make Season 17 especially significant. Ziva is understandably hurt that Gibbs never searched for her after she was presumed dead, and the two have to confront what that absence meant to them. They have to work through the resentment, which makes their eventual reconciliation feel earned.

When it came to their relationship, Tony and Ziva had chemistry from the moment they began working together, although neither was particularly eager to admit it. Their early dynamic was full of teasing and competitive banter, but Season 3's "Under Covers" gave viewers a memorable glimpse of what lay beneath. The pair go undercover as a couple, complete with a romantic rendezvous, and the episode helped establish the attraction that would become one of the show's longest-running storylines.

What made Tony and Ziva work wasn't constant flirting. They were colleagues who understood each other's instincts, challenged one another, and developed a loyalty that extended well beyond the job. Their relationship took years to evolve, and even when they weren't saying what they felt, the audience could usually tell.

Their eventual daughter, Tali, brought that story into a new chapter. After Ziva was presumed dead following an attack on her farmhouse, Tony left NCIS to raise their daughter. Ziva's return in Season 17 revealed she was alive, allowing the series to revisit a relationship left unresolved for years. The pair eventually reunited in Paris, and their story continued in the Paramount+ spinoff NCIS: Tony & Ziva.

The spinoff's cancellation after one season left Tony and Ziva's story in an uncertain place, but the flagship series hasn't exactly forgotten about them. Tony's return in Season 24 has brought Ziva back into the conversation, even though she isn't appearing on screen. Tony tells McGee and Nick Torres (Wilmer Valderrama) that she's in the Boston area, working at a language institute while keeping an eye on Tali, who has started boarding school nearby. He even suggests she could be available if the team needs her.

Showrunner Steven D. Binder has said Ziva won't appear in Season 24, so that update isn't confirmation of an imminent reunion. Still, it's telling that the show takes the time to establish where she is and what she's doing. Her life remains relevant to Tony's, and Tony's life remains relevant to the show.

Bishop (Emily Wickersham), Knight (Katrina Law), and the agents who followed Ziva have brought their own strengths to NCIS. The series doesn't need another Mossad operative to make the team interesting, and trying to recreate Ziva would probably be a mistake. What it hasn't found is another character whose personal history could influence so many corners of the show at once. Ziva's story was about more than becoming a better agent. It was about a woman trained to serve other people's expectations finally deciding what she wanted for herself. She found a family in the people who once questioned whether she belonged, and built a future that looked nothing like the one her father had planned. NCIS can fill another desk, but it can't manufacture that history overnight, and judging by how often the show still brings Ziva into the conversation, it hasn't stopped trying to find a way to bring her back.

Follows the Major Case Response Team (MCRT) from the Naval Criminal Investigative Service (NCIS), as they get to the bottom of criminal cases connected to Navy and Marine Corps personnel.

M. Night Shyamalan's The Visit: Now Free on YouTube

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Just don't leave your room after 9:30 P.M.

Though Halloween is over, that doesn't mean that horror movies need to be put on the shelf until next year. In fact, it could even be argued that November is a perfect time to continue watching them. Writer/director M. Night Shyamalan has no shortage of horror films that can work in the chilly, dreary days of November, such as 2004's The Village. But an underrated 2015 found-footage horror film manages to cement itself in Shyamalan's filmography as one of his greatest unexpected achievements.

The Visit was released in 2015, following a downward streak in Shyamalan's career. During this time, found footage was experiencing a boom, so Shyamalan's decision to capitalize on that public interest made sense, even if that wasn't the kind of horror film one would expect from him. It grossed $98.5 million worldwide on a $5 million budget, and it received widespread praise from critics. However, it seemed to slowly fade from public consciousness — now, eleven years later, there is a chance to revisit The Visit.

The Visit stars Olivia DeJonge and Ed Oxenbould as siblings Becca and Tyler. When their recently-divorced mother Loretta (Kathryn Hahn) goes on a five-day cruise, she leaves the kids in the care of her estranged grandparents, whom she hasn't spoken to in fifteen years because they disapproved of her marriage to her high school teacher. Becca and Tyler decide to use the opportunity to make a documentary of the visit with a handheld camcorder.

Nana (Deanna Dunagan) and Pop Pop (Peter McRobbie) meet Becca and Tyler at the train station. Initially, it seems as if everything is fine. They even wonder why their mom disliked them so much. Once they get to the house, though, they are told to never leave their room after 9:30 P.M. and to never go into the basement. This immediately sets the children on edge and creates the simmering tension that makes up the film.

Quickly, Nana and Pop Pop go from pleasant to unsettling. Each time Becca and Tyler witness something — especially at night — it carries a foreboding, sickening sense of anticipation. Pop Pop attacks a man that he thinks is following them, and Nana chases the children during a game of hide-and-seek. Tyler finds soiled diapers in the shed, and Becca sees Nana vomiting violently in the downstairs hallway. When challenged, the grandparents are each dismissive of the behavior of the other. As things begin to escalate, Becca and Tyler are on their own as they try to figure out what to do with two people they barely even know.

The film drew praise not just for its scary moments, but for the moments where it lets itself have a laugh or two. The terror it draws from lies within the everyday, which is a perfect setting for a director like Shyamalan, whose twists have become an integral part of his filmmaking signature. A single-house setting with limited boundaries adds claustrophobia and helps The Visit stand out from its peers.

With The Visit streaming for free on YouTube, it's the perfect time to give this film a chance and see for yourself what, exactly, is going on with Nana and Pop Pop.

Freddie Prinze Jr. & Sarah Michelle Gellar’s Son, 14, Drives Porsche

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Freddie Prinze Jr. and Sarah Michelle Gellar's 14-year-old son Rocky shared his latest zooming adventure: getting the chance to race around in a Porsche.

Rocky Prinze is ready to lap the competition.

The 14-year-old son of Freddie Prinze Jr. and Sarah Michelle Gellar has been deep into racing over the past year and took his skills to a new level as he got behind the wheel of a Porsche.

Rocky posted several photos of himself with a gray Porsche coupe to Instagram Nov. 6, sharing a quote by auto founder Ferdinand Porsche reading, "In the beginning I looked around and could not find quite the car I dreamed of. So I decided to build it myself.”

Rocky captioned the monumental moment, "My first time driving one.”

In the pics—shared in a joint post with his dad—the teen posed at the wheel, as well as on top of and next to the fast car.

And his parents have been revving up their support for his racing dreams, with Freddie posting about Rocky’s ambitions in March. The She’s All That star shared a video of his son speeding around a track on Instagram, writing, “Day 1. My son Rocky putting in that work on his dream.”

And it’s been his goal to get fast and furious from a very young age, with Rocky even sharing a snap of a project he did as a little kid in which he said he really likes “to race cars.” He captioned the August Instagram pic, “Living my kindergarten dream.”

Sarah—who also shares daughter Charlotte, 17, with Freddie—encouraged her followers to keep up with her son as he zooms into a new chapter, posting a photo of him on a go-kart and saying, “We know you’ve heard us talk about Rocky racing… but now you can follow his competition journey.”

"How do we foster that for him? I don't know a lot about F1 and racing, so it's like finding those contacts," she told People in March. "And luckily, I worked with someone who's quite a prominent racer—Patrick Dempsey."

16 New & Returning DC Shows That Could Be Affected by James Gunn’s Exit

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The DC Universe and the larger DC Studios are going through a seismic shift now, and this could impact several new and returning TV shows. James Gunn and Peter Safran are out at DC, with the duo no longer overseeing their announced DCU Chapter One slate, which has released movies like Superman and Supergirl as well as shows like Lanterns and Creature Commandos.

Gunn and Safran will be replaced by former Marvel executive Jeremy Latcham, who worked with Kevin Feige right from the start of the Marvel Cinematic Universe with the release of 2008's Iron Man. Given the recent change, Latcham has yet to reveal what his vision for the DCU and the projects outside the shared universe entails. That could mean either keeping some planned returning and new DC TV shows or completely scrapping them, with shows to note including:

Creature Commandos season 2 is one of the upcoming DC shows to be among the safe releases at the studio, Gunn or not. The adult animated series' first season was well-received, standing at a near-perfect 93% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes, and work on season 2 is well underway. As recently as October 24, Gunn confirmed that Creature Commandos season 2 would debut on HBO Max after Man of Tomorrow, so in the second half of 2027.

Mister Miracle is another adult animated DC series that should have no problem making it to the small screen in 2027. The show is based on one of the most acclaimed comics books in recent memory, with the writer of that DC Comics series, Tom King, also serving as Mister Miracle's showrunner. Additionally, DC recently confirmed the castings of The Hunger Games franchise star Josh Hutcherson as Mister Miracle and Below actress Mackenzie Davis as Big Barda, so walking back on those castings would not look good. Finally, Darkseid will make his DCU debut in the show, making it essential.

The People v. Gorilla Grodd is in slightly shakier territory than the other two previous DCU series. While it is a live-action series that has already wrapped production, and thus would not make much sense financially to scrap, Warner Bros. Discovery previously shelved a full Batgirl movie with Michael Keaton as Batman and Brendan Fraser as the villain. As such, if the new regime deems The People v. Gorilla Grodd too silly for the DC brand in live-action, there is a chance it could get scrapped. Still, given the many DCU returning characters, expect it in 2027 for now.

HBO's Lanterns not being renewed by now lit up a warning sign, given how the live-action DC show broke viewership records throughout season 1 and stands at a stellar 92% critics' score on Rotten Tomatoes. It is true that many DC fans complained about making a Green Lantern series grounded and more of a gritty detective show than a cosmic superhero adventure. Additionally, Lanterns season 1 wrapped up its story with a couple of loose ends, setting up John Stewart for Gunn's Man of Tomorrow. Lanterns season 2 feels like it could go either way.

Peacemaker season 3 is a DC project that fans might want to keep off their minds for the time being, as it is very unlikely to happen. Gunn himself said at the end of season 2 that there were no active plans for another season. With the creative behind the show not out of the DC Studios co-CEO job, the new regime should not bring in someone else to take over Peacemaker, a project that is so connected to Gunn's creative DNA.

The Penguin season 2 is most likely dead at DC Studios despite being loved by fans and critics alike. No significant movement was made on a possible new season with Gunn still in charge, and with Skydance now looking at franchises, to get the DCU Batman up and running, there is a strong chance Matt Reeves' long-delayed The Batman universe plans will call it a day with 2028's The Batman - Part II.

Poison Ivy was one of the most recent DC shows to be reported to enter development. In late October, The Penguin creator Lauren LeFranc was revealed to be developing a live-action Poison Ivy series set outside The Batman universe, thus likely in Gunn's DCU. Given the modest success of Clayface on the big screen so far, Poison Ivy might have to fight an uphill battle despite The Penguin being a hit at HBO, with focus likely to shift to bigger characters.

The animated Harley Quinn series starring Kaley Cuoco as the Batman character exists since before Gunn's tenure at DC. The show's long-running success on HBO Max and the fact that it is both animated and set within its own universe with spin-off Kite Man: Hell Yeah!, means a renewal might not conflict with the new DCU plans. That said, its fate was not a priority for DC Studios with Gunn, and it should not be one now with Latcham, so the limbo period could be lengthy.

Batman: Caped Crusader was dropped by HBO Max during development, with the DC series spearheaded by Batman: The Animated Series' Bruce Timm later moving to Prime Video. The show received a two-season order at Prime Video, with season 2 having debuted this year. It remains to be seen what Prime Video thinks about another season, but as far as the new DC goes, the show is unlikely to be a priority. With Matt Reeves attached as an executive producer, maybe that helps it continue.

My Adventures with Green Lantern is one of the recent DC animated series to enter development, being a spin-off to My Adventures with Superman. Speaking to ScreenRant in June, one of the showrunners of the Superman series, showrunners Jake Wyatt, claimed that the new show's release was pending definition from the Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount merger. While development is moving along, this predicament puts it in danger.

How the American French Film Festival Is Bringing French Cinema to 3,000 Los Angeles High School Students With Packed Screenings of ‘In Waves’

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

The American French Film Festival, which celebrated its 30th anniversary this year, has been on a journey to cultivate a new generation of French movie lovers for two decades with its educational program for Los Angeles high school students.

This milestone edition brought around 3,000 students to the DGA theater for five packed screenings of “In Waves,” Phuong Mai Nguyen’s heart-wrenching animated feature which was bought Netflix at Cannes. Related Stories These Coffee Table Books Make the Perfect Gift This Holiday Season Sacha Baron Cohen Says Marvel Worried Casting a Jewish Actor as the Devil in ‘Ironheart’ Would Be Antisemitic

Adapted from AJ Dungo’s graphic novel at set in Southern California, “In Waves” is a love story between a shy teenage boy and the girl who teaches him how to surf and how to live, before she succumbs to cancer.

The screenings, which drew applause and tears throughout, also gave students the opportunity to engage in the post-screening Q&A with the film’s producer, Priscilla Bertin, and screenwriter Fanny Burdino.

Spearheaded by Anouchka Van Riel, the festival deputy director, the program brings together more than 60 schools over five days, with roughly half coming from the public school system and half from private institutions. It also reaches beyond French-language classes to students studying performing arts and other creative disciplines.

In an interview with Variety, Van Riel discussed the importance of the programme to allow younger audiences to discover international cinema at a time when arthouse moviegoers are aging and foreign-language films face growing challenges in U.S. theaters. She also talked about the program’s rapid growth, why subtitles are no longer the barrier they once were, and the powerful response to “In Waves” which Netflix is positioning in the Awards race.

How many students and schools participate in the program?

We have more than 60 schools participating over five days, so that’s more than 12 schools a day. We organize five screenings of the same film, each with around 600 students. That’s approximately 3,000 students every year. I’ve built a database of around 300 schools, and we start reaching out in May. Within a week of sending the first email, half the seats — around 1,500 — are already booked. Then I spend the summer filling the remaining places. There’s enormous demand. We also make a point of having a mix of public and private schools, roughly 50/50. That’s very important to us. For public schools, transportation can be a challenge. Some have to apply for grants just to pay for the buses. There’s an entire economy around organizing these trips, and a lot depends on the teachers.

How does the festival work with teachers to prepare students for the screenings?

We work with the American Association of Teachers of French, which prepares two educational guides, one in French and one in English. The French-language guide is around 100 pages long. It’s incredibly detailed and allows teachers to explore the film from multiple perspectives, including its themes and cinematic language. The guides are designed to help students study the film before or after the screening. And we don’t just work with French-language classes. Around half of the participating classes are English-speaking. Many Los Angeles high schools have performing arts programs, and those students come because they’re interested in cinema and want to meet the filmmakers. The majority of these kids have never attended a film screening followed by a Q&A. That’s something we want to change.

That’s surprising in Los Angeles, where the film industry is everywhere.

It’s extraordinary. I’m a member of the Producers Guild, and I receive invitations to screenings and conversations with filmmakers almost every day. There are incredible events happening constantly, but these kids don’t have access to them. Of course, if their parents work in the industry, that’s different. But otherwise, most students aren’t exposed to that world. I grew up in a small town in Alsace, and I remember my French teacher taking us to the movies all the time when I was in middle school. Some of those screenings even had Q&As. There’s a tradition of that in France that doesn’t really exist here in the same way. We used to organize masterclasses for university students, including at USC, but we realized it was difficult to attract them. They’re already surrounded by so many opportunities on campus. With high school students, we can work directly through the teachers. That’s how we’ve developed this program.

Is the goal ultimately to cultivate a younger audience for French cinema?

Absolutely. It’s about renewing the audience. A couple of years ago, we screened “The Marching Band,” and when the film was eventually released theatrically in the U.S., I was invited to moderate a Q&A. I loved the film, so I was very happy to do it. But the theater was only half full, and I don’t think there was a single person under 65. That’s the problem. Foreign-language films have traditionally been considered niche in the U.S. Streaming platforms have helped change that perception somewhat, but we still need to familiarize younger audiences with these films. That’s why this program is so important.

Are subtitles still an obstacle for younger American audiences?

Not nearly as much as people think. We’ve conducted surveys with students after the screenings, and more than 80% say subtitles aren’t a barrier to watching foreign-language films. It’s not a completely scientific study, but it gives us a good indication of how attitudes are changing. Between streaming platforms, social media and series like “Squid Game,” young people have become accustomed to watching subtitled content. Sometimes there’s still a little hesitation before the screening. But once they’re sitting in the theater, they realize it’s easy. I really don’t think subtitles are the problem for this generation anymore. Distribution is a major part of the problem. That’s why streaming platforms have become so important for the circulation of foreign-language films. But there’s also the question of exposure. If young people aren’t given opportunities to discover these films, they’re not necessarily going to seek them out themselves.

This year’s selection was “In Waves.” What made it particularly suitable for the program?

There’s a wonderful connection between this film and the festival. The producer, Priscilla Bertin, had already come to the festival two years ago with “Wild Diamond,” and at the time she was in post-production on “In Waves.” We became familiar with the project very early, and it made complete sense for us to bring it back this year. What’s interesting is that the film is based on an American graphic novel that was discovered by a French producer in a secondhand bookstore in France and developed into a movie. We explain that background to the students before the screenings, including why it’s considered a French film and the response has been extraordinary.

The screenings were packed, and the students seemed particularly moved by the film. What was the atmosphere like?

It was very striking. At the first screening, I went straight into introducing the filmmakers for the Q&A, and I realized something wasn’t right because the students were still processing what they’d just watched. Usually, when a film ends, the students immediately line up to ask questions. But with “In Waves,” it took time. Every day, there was this moment of silence. The film had affected them so deeply that they needed a little time before they could speak. There’s something cathartic about sitting in a dark theater with other people, crying together and sharing the same emotions. You feel safe because everyone is experiencing that grief together. I think young people have fewer opportunities to experience cinema that way now. That’s what made these screenings so special.

3 Elite Prime Video Series to Binge This Week (Nov 9-13)

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

As November enters its second week, David DiGilio’s The Terminal List is still the number one TV show on Prime Video. Based on the novels by Jack Carr, the action thriller series stars Chris Pratt as US Navy SEAL James Reece, with the newly released Season 2 following Reece on the run after being placed on the FBI Most Wanted List. The season has been performing quite admirably ever since its premiere in late October, but that’s hardly the only good show worth watching on the platform. Here’s a look at three great shows that we think you should binge this week on Prime Video.

For more recommendations, check out our list of the best shows and movies on Prime Video.

An action-adventure comedy series created by Tessa Coates, Ride or Die follows best friends Debbie and Judith. Debbie is a quiet, former attorney turned devoted wife to a Member of the British Parliament, and Judith is a rule-breaking international assassin living as an accountant. Following a surprise divorce, Debbie is suddenly pulled into Judith’s dangerous, secret world as they find themselves on the run from a mysterious enemy. Hannah Waddingham and Octavia Spencer star as the mismatched duo, with Ed Skrein, Calam Lynch, Jamie Parker, Sylvia Hoeks, and Bill Nighy as key characters.

Ride or Die is a high-energy, high-stakes action-comedy caper that blends espionage with road trips and messy female friendships. The series makes for a fun and breezy watch with its classic buddy-comedy tropes and enjoyable performances by Spencer and Waddingham. On its premiere, Ride or Die ranked among the top Prime Video original shows of 2026, earning widespread critical acclaim for the chemistry between its female leads and a 96% on Rotten Tomatoes, but it was still canceled after just one season.

Created by Graham Wagner and Geneva Robertson-Dworet, Fallout is Prime Video’s adaptation of Tim Cain and Leonard Boyarsky’s popular role-playing video game franchise of the same name. Set in the same official canon and timeline as the video game, in the aftermath of a nuclear holocaust, the series focuses on three main characters whose paths intersect in a devastated Los Angeles while chasing a revolutionary cold fusion technology. The series stars Ella Purnell, Aaron Moten, Kyle MacLachlan, Moisés Arias, and Walton Goggins as main characters.

Since its premiere in 2024, Fallout has secured its position among the best sci-fi shows of our time. The series has since been very popular among franchise fans for its faithfulness to the tone and style of the original game’s visuals, character design, and settings, making it feel more like an extension of the game than just a translation. Critics have hailed Fallout as one of the best video game adaptations of all time, rivaling The Last of Us and Arcane, with the series winning two Creative Arts Emmy Awards and two Emmy nominations so far.

One of the newest dramas on Prime Video, Sterling Point is created by Megan Park and follows Annie Jacobson, whose life takes a sudden turn when she and her twin brother, Connor, inherit a home in Canada. When they arrive at their grandfather’s cottage, Annie meets a local girl and co-heir to the property, and spends a summer exploring new romances and untold family secrets. Ella Rubin, Amélie Hoeferle, Jacob Whiteduck-Lavoie, Daniel Quinn-Toye, and Keen Ruffalo play the main characters.

Renewed for a second season alongside its series premiere, Sterling Point has quickly become a highly rated original romance drama on Prime Video. The show has been praised for perfectly capturing the charm and harsh realities of the current adolescent generation, balancing romantic pursuits and sexual awakenings with the harder truths and emotional complexities of personal growth. Sterling Point has also been favorably compared to genre classics like Dawson’s Creek and another Prime Video hit, The Summer I Turned Pretty.

8 Years Later, Marvel's King In Black Finally Meets Someone Stronger

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

This post contains a PREVIEW for Godzilla Conquers The Multiverse #5

For nearly a decade, Knull the Symbiote God has established himself as one of Marvel Comics' most powerful and dangerous villains, though it seems he's finally met his match.

Having officially debuted eight years ago in the pages of Marvel Comics' King in Black event, Knull is a primordial force who created and commands armies of living darkness (symbiotes). Seeking to return the universe to the abyss in defiance of the light, it's pretty surprising to discover a being more powerful than Knull thanks to one of Marvel's upcoming crossover events.

Releasing later this week, Marvel Comics will publish Godzilla Conquers The Multiverse #5 from Gerry Duggan and Javier Garron, the final chapter in a major kaiju crossover that has seen King in Black and King of the Monsters joining forces for a huge multiversal rampage. However, a new preview has just revealed the seemingly more powerful than Knull himself in this final battle: Mothra.

As seen in the preview pages for Godzilla Conquers The Multiverse #5, the final battle between Doctor Doom's Avengers and Knull and Godzilla is well underway. Having gathered their own kaiju allies from across the multiverse in earlier issues, the lethal combo of Wolverine and Godzilla named Weapon 'Zilla is unleashed by Ghost Rider, along with Miles Morales' pet kaiju Omeg, who's since grown into a major powerhouse. However, the team of unlikely survivors is also backed by Mothra, with Doom surprisingly placing his utmost confidence in Mothra to handle Knull on her own.

Choose the movie with the higher Rotten Tomatoes score.

Sign in to claim your place on the leaderboard!

Sure enough, the giant kaiju successfully shrugs off Knull's normally overwhelming corruption and symbiotes as if they were nothing before using her own light to completely overpower his darkness and bind the King in Black. While it's possible that Knull could perhaps escape later on in the full issue, it does seem like he's been well and truly defeated, with all attention likely turning to Godzilla himself as the much bigger threat.

At any rate, Mothra easily resisting and overpowering Knull is pretty remarkable. After all, this is a villain who once tore the Sentry in half like he was paper. Apparently, her light is a natural counter to the King in Black's darkness, making Mothra the perfect foil in this new crossover series, and it will be fascinating to see what else she might accomplish when the full issue hits store shelves.

Beyond Godzilla Conquers the Multiverse, 2026 has been a pretty great year for Godzilla fans overall.

Firstly, this current Marvel crossover follows Godzilla: Infinity Roar, as well as DC's major Justice League vs. Godzilla vs. Kong 2 series. On the screen, Godzilla Minus Zero has just been released in theaters, and the end of the month will see the release of the first-ever Godzilla LEGO set, featuring over 5,300 pieces and standing over 70cm tall.

Combined with confirmed releases in the future like next year's Godzilla x Kong: Supernova and another season of Monarch: Legacy of Monsters in the works, the Godzilla franchise is clearly stronger than ever.​​​​​​​

Godzilla Conquers The Multiverse #5 releases November 11th from Marvel Comics.

Robert Pattinson's James Bond Comments Resurface 14 Years Later Amid Casting Rumors

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Fresh off his electric performance in the dark, unsettling media satire "Primetime," Robert Pattinson is said to be in contention for the role of James Bond. According to The Hollywood Reporter, Pattinson screen tested for the part in Denis Villeneuve's upcoming Bond reboot movie for Amazon MGM Studios, which puts him in the running alongside several other well-known contenders. For the actor, it has been a long time coming. It seems that, 14 years ago, Pattinson envisioned stepping into the 007 role at a later point in his career.

In a 2012 interview with The Sun (via NDTV), the British star said he'd "definitely like to go for Bond" but wanted to wait another 20 years. "There'd be nothing worse than, like, 'Let's get a fresh-faced Bond!'" he added. "That would be the worst idea in the world. It would be ridiculous to reinvent it as some young posh kid." At the time, he was just starting to make his transition from teen heartthrob to chameleonic leading man, and was promoting what has since become known as one of Pattinson's best movies, David Cronenberg's "Cosmopolis." Since then, much has changed, and while it hasn't quite been two decades, it seems the actor wasn't kidding when he said he'd one day like to play 007.

Back in October 2026, it was reported that Josh O'Connor was one of the Bond finalists, alongside "Fantastic Beasts" actor Callum Turner, and "Slow Horses" star Jack Lowden. Since then, reports have claimed that Nicholas Hoult and Pattinson are also in the final group, which means five actors are now vying for the role of England's greatest spy. If Pattinson does land the role, it will be yet another major success for the actor and, given his prior comments, a particularly satisfying one.

In his interview with The Sun, Robert Pattinson seemed entirely opposed to the idea of a young James Bond. "After Daniel Craig you have to have some baggage," he said of the erstwhile Bond actor, who reinvented the spy for a new generation with the unbeatable "Casino Royale" in 2006. "I'd have to be tortured in the first few scenes. I'd have to do the first film with one arm or something." It's unclear just how serious Pattinson was at the time, but he seemed to have an awareness of just how tortured a version of Bond Craig portrayed during his tenure in the tux.

With that in mind, it will be interesting to see, if Pattinson does land the part, whether he doubles down on the haunted loner version of 007 he mentioned in his 2012 comments. The best Bonds have all had an edge to them, but it might be that Denis Villeneuve and Amazon MGM want to go in a different direction after the diminishing returns of the Craig era Bond films.

As it stands, nobody knows for sure who will play the lead role in Villeneuve's upcoming film. Based on the finalists, one thing we do know is that Amazon won't be going the "young posh kid" route, to use Pattinson's phrasing. All the contenders are 36 years old, except for Pattinson who at 40 is the oldest of the bunch. With final screen tests said to have already taken place, it won't be long before we get an official announcement, and if Pattinson does land the role we at least know he's been mentally preparing for 14 years.

→ Baca artikel asli

Colin Farrell's Remake Of A '90s Sci-Fi Movie Is One Of Prime Video's Biggest Streaming Hits

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

"Total Recall" (2012) is directed by Len Wiseman, who most recently oversaw the 2025 "John Wick" offshoot "Ballerina" and previously helmed the "Underworld" films between 2003 and 2016. He is, unfortunately, also responsible for 2007's "Live Free or Die Hard," which, despite making the third spot on /Film's "Die Hard" movie ranking, we simply could have done without.

Unlike the 1990 original, "Total Recall" is set entirely on an Earth ravaged by chemical war, leaving only the United Federation of Britain (UFB) and the Colony (Australia) habitable. Whereas Arnold Schwarzenegger's Douglas Quaid had dreams of Mars, which turn out to be long-buried memories, Colin Farrell's counterpart finds himself experiencing nocturnal visions of being a secret agent. Bored of his factory job on The Colony, he enlists the company Rekall to implant artificial memories to match his dreams, which he soon learns are, in fact, memories of having worked as a UFB agent. Hunted by his former employers, he teams up with Jessica Biel's Melina, a member of a rebel group known as The Resistance. But as the pair try to outrun the UFB and their Chancellor Vilos Cohaagen (Bryan Cranston), the line between fantasy and reality becomes increasingly blurred.

Ahead of the film's debut, /Film asked: "Is 'Total Recall' the least essential film of the summer?" The answer, according to most critics, was yes. But it seems the Prime Video crowds are willing to give it another shot. Per streaming viewership tracker FlixPatrol, "Total Recall" is the No. 3 most-watched movie on Prime Video in the United States as of November 9, 2026. What's more, based on the film's trajectory thus far, it could be set for a chart-topping run. "Total Recall" debuted on the charts at No. 9 on November 7, 2026, before jumping to fourth the following day and hitting No. 3 the day after. By the end of the week, Farrell's forgotten remake might just hit No. 1 — not bad for a film with a 30% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes.

Both Philip K. Dick's story and the 1990 "Total Recall" film feature Mars. But the 2012 remake actually has more in common with Paul Verhoeven's rendition than the novel, using characters that only appear in the movie and adhering more closely to the big-screen storyline. That might have seemed like a reasonable approach, given how the first film was so successful. Unfortunately, Len Wiseman's effort didn't emulate the offbeat comedy of the original, which was so sharply rendered by Arnold Schwarzenegger and gave the film such a unique tone. Despite featuring some impressive action scenes, Wiseman's version also managed to feel like a generic sci-fi actioner, all of which combined to yield a less-than-impressive critical response.

That said, Roger Ebert was impressed enough to bestow three stars on the film — just half a star less than the 1990 version — and called it a "well-crafted, high-energy sci-fi." Still, even he had to admit that the film was one of several sci-fi movie remakes that should have never happened, ending his review by writing, "It never touched me emotionally, though, the way the 1990 film did, and strictly speaking, isn't necessary." Others were much more condemnatory, with David Sexton of the London Evening Standard giving the film one star and writing, "We used to do the future so much better. Let's not forget that. Let's forget this instead."

Normally, that should deter you from joining the "Total Recall" renaissance on Prime Video. But considering that, since the movie's debut, we've been subjected to an onslaught of streaming movie shabbiness, it might be worth a go to see if the remake really is bad or whether that's merely a memory implanted by the critics.

Roger Ebert Called Disney's Attempt To Update A Classic Cartoon Character 'Transcendently Bad'

📅 Nov 10, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Kids of today likely won't have heard of the visually-impaired cartoon millionaire known as Mr. Magoo. But for a large part of the 20th Century he was a pop culture fixture who entertained generations of kids with his slapstick shenanigans. By 1997, however, his appeal had waned, which makes Disney's decision to reboot the IP with a Leslie Nielsen-led live-action film all the more confounding. Thankfully, Roger Ebert was on-hand to let the studio have it — which he dutifully did with a scathing review that, bizarrely enough, actually awarded the film half a star.

The critic handed out some damning reviews in his time. Ebert hated Leonard Nimoy's box office flop "Funny About Love" and even walked out of the Oscar-winning war movie "Mediterraneo." All of which left filmmakers terrified of being handed the dreaded "thumbs down" for their efforts. Around 60 movies earned this mark of shame during the critic's career but somehow, "Mr. Magoo" managed to eke out half a star. If you read Ebert's thoughts on the film you'll no doubt wonder how it managed such a feat.

In his review, Ebert revealed that the only thing he liked about the film was the titular character's red Studebaker — that was it. Otherwise, he considered "Mr. Magoo" to be so bad it "soars above ordinary badness as the eagle outreaches the fly." How, then, did this ill-advised comedy reboot manage to get a half-star out of the old boy? Did that Studebaker really save "Mr. Magoo" from the thumbs down club? It's hard to imagine given just how much Ebert hated this movie.

There are plenty of movies Roger Ebert hated that are worth watching, but "Mr. Magoo" isn't one of them. The character first appeared in the 1949 theatrical short "The Ragtime Bear," where he was voiced by none other than future "Gilligan's Island" star and legendary character actor Jim Backus. Backus voiced the United Productions of America (UPA) character until 1977, portraying him in theatrical cartoons, TV series, and TV specials. While Mr. Magoo's character developed over the course of his existence, he's best known as a lovably oblivious old millionaire, whose extreme nearsightedness sees him wander into dangerous situations only to miraculously escape unharmed — causing significant distress to his nephew and frequent sidekick Waldo.

While Backus stopped voicing the character after the 1977 TV series "What's New, Mr. Magoo?" the visually-impaired millionaire endured. The most recent incarnation of the character appeared in the "Mr. Magoo" cartoon, which wrapped up in 2023. Prior to that, however, Disney decided that kids of the mid-'90s needed to be reintroduced to Quincy Magoo, and hired Hong Kong filmmaker Stanley Tong to make it happen.

In what turned out to be Tong's one and only English-language film, the frequent Jackie Chan collaborator teamed up not with a martial arts legend but with Leslie Nielsen. The "Naked Gun" star played the eponymous millionaire in a film that saw him hunted by the FBI and criminals eager to recover a priceless gem to which Mr. Magoo is entirely oblivious. Much of the movie follows the tried-and-true formula of its title character getting himself into absurd situations and coming out unscathed, such as when he commandeers an ironing board to ski after thieves. The movie made $28.9 million on a $30 million budget and upset Roger Ebert considerably.

In his half-star review of "Mr. Magoo," Roger Ebert described the film as being "transcendently bad," and accused it of being entirely without laughs. The critic admitted to never having been a fan of the original cartoons, which he recalled as essentially telling the same joke over and over. Likewise, the film was, in Ebert's estimation, "a one-joke movie without the joke."

Interestingly enough, this might be the only time he ever took aim at the bloopers, which play during the film's credits and evidently failed to amuse the critic. "Even the outtakes at the end aren't funny," he wrote, "and I'm not sure I understood one of them, unless it was meant to show stunt people hilariously almost being drowned." Ebert wasn't even impressed with Leslie Nielsen, who'd long since established himself as a comedic force but was, according to the reviewer, "unable to overcome the fact that a little Magoo at six minutes in a cartoon is a far different matter than a lot of Magoo at 90 minutes in a feature."

On "Siskel & Ebert," neither of the hosts could bring themselves to say anything nice about the movie. Gene Siskel called it "uninspired" before Ebert took the opportunity to once again lay into the movie, calling it a "wet blanket." Roger Ebert was wrong about a fair few movies in his time, but a 10% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes suggests he was right on the money with this one. And yet, he gave the movie half a star. Why? Did the Studebaker really save it from the shame of a thumbs down rating?

Ever since its mid-August debut, Damon Lindelof and Tom King's adaptation of the Green Lantern comics has proved a major hit for HBO. Giants get first win in Philadelphia since 2013

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Sunday’s performance in Philadelphia wasn’t a work of art for the Giants, but it was more than good enough to end their long losing streak in the City of Brotherly Love.

Tight end Isaiah Likely’s touchdown catch in the third quarter gave the Giants their first lead since the first quarter and they expanded it to 19-11 before punting the ball back to the Eagles with 90 seconds left to play in the game. Joe Milton picked up first downs on completions to Hollywood Brown and Dallas Goedert, but rookie wideout Makai Lemon dropped a pass that would have moved them into Giants territory and running back Elijah Mitchell didn’t get out of bounds on a short catch on the next play.

Milton spiked the ball with six seconds left to play and the Eagles on their own 44-yard-line for one final heave. Milton famously uncorked a 70-yard throw at the Combine, but he never got a chance to throw the ball because he was sacked by defensive end Darius Alexander.

That was one of seven Giants sacks on the afternoon, including three from first-round pick Arvell Reese. It was Reese’s first road game against the Eagles and his 1-0 record in Philly is a big change from recent history. The Giants had lost 13 straight games in Philadelphia and the route to ending that skid featured some strange twists and turns.

Likely’s touchdown catch came on a throw from Jameis Winston, who replaced J.J. McCarthy after McCarthy hurt his hip in the third quarter. Winston played one full series and threw for 71 yards on his way to the touchdown, but McCarthy returned just before the end of the quarter and led the offense for the rest of the proceedings.

McCarthy was sacked five times, but was 12-of-19 for 127 yards when he had time to throw and he ran for 22 yards while evading pressure over the course of the afternoon. The Giants couldn’t find any other success on the ground and Sunday’s 243 yards of offense didn’t make a strong case for the Giants sitting out the trade deadline if they have a chance to acquire any kind of help for their backfield.

Wide receiver Malik Nabers had five catches for 43 yards, but dropped a pass in the end zone that would have iced the win and his body language was that of an unhappy player throughout the game. The win should make it easier to avoid it being a focal point for the team after the game, but Nabers will likely have to answer some questions about what was bugging him.

They’ll be back on the field Thursday night against the Commanders and another win would move them to 6-2-1 with a 3-0-1 record in the division. That would make them even heavier favorites to win an NFC East that is theirs for the taking.

The Eagles spent a lot of time in that position in recent years, but injuries have gutted the roster and Milton didn’t provide any significant upgrade on the offensive production that the team got with Tanner McKee at quarterback. The Eagles will have a bye week to determine which way they want to go in the future, but the 2-7 team should really only have eyes on 2027 at this point.

Recasting Sabrina The Teenage Witch In 2026: All 9 Main Roles

📅 Nov 08, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

In 1996, Melissa Joan Hart perfectly embodied the titular star of the magical coming-of-age sitcom, Sabrina the Teenage Witch, but 30 years on, a reboot could have a very different cast. The show was a massive success, and as such, it ended up running for seven seasons from 1996 until 2003.

Beyond that, the series also ended up with an animated spin-off and sequel, a reimagined revival in 2018 which took a much darker approach to the narrative, and a handful of feature films, including the 1996 movie starring Hart and Ryan Reynolds that kicked it all off.

But, if TV executives wanted to bring the series back today, and stick more closely to the light-hearted comedy roots of the 1996 series, there are only a handful of actors who could really excel at playing the parts that brought this series to life in the first place.

First up, we need a loud, boisterous, and versatile actor who can bring the humor, enthusiasm, and depth to the character of Sabrina that this role deserves. And with that in mind, there is no one better suited to playing this part than Ruby Stokes. Stokes has been steadily building out her credits since 2010, with a major turn in 2016 when she played a central role in the film Una.

Since then, Stokes has tackled a variety of performances, from the stage to the screen, and performed in period dramas, dark comedies, and captivating dramas, all of which have given her both the experience to play the role and more than qualified her to take the lead as a mischievous young witch discovering her powers.

While Gillian Jacobs played the uptight and anxious Britta in Community, the role that she is currently best known for, she also has the capacity to play much more laid-back and fun-loving roles. And considering Hilda Spellman, one of Sabrina's aunts who is also a full witch, is prone to act on impulse and take things less seriously than her sister Zelda, it would make a lot of sense to cast Jacobs as Hilda.

Hilda is the younger sister who loves to have fun and is willing to play along with Sabrina's ideas, often getting herself in trouble with her sister along with their niece. Jacobs brings humor, vibrancy, and warmth to her roles that allows her to deliver characters who may not be the most practical, but they are certainly endearing.

Meanwhile, Zelda Spellman is frequently the voice of reason, and the most level-headed witch in the room. In order to bring this part to life, someone like Ayo Edebiri, who recently came off her turn in the incredible series, The Bear, would be an ideal choice to play the person keeping everything together while everyone else is chasing their own directions.

In many ways, this is exactly how her character, Sydney, ended her run in The Bear, having learned valuable lessons through her challenging work with the team at The Bear restaurant, before taking the reins and becoming the Head Chef and co-owner. So, in a sense, playing Zelda could help to see that type of character evolve even further in Edebiri's capable hands.

Bill Hader is an accomplished comedy actor whose deadpan delivery style has made him a fan favorite throughout his career, from his time as a regular cast member on SNL to a series of roles in TV and film over the last decade or so. Hader has also taken to voice acting in recent years with a growing list of credits for major roles in projects such as The Cat in The Hat, Inside Out, and many more.

With all of that preparation, Hader is easily the strongest candidate to tackle the sarcastic and snappy wizard who was turned into a cat as a punishment for attempting to take over the world, Salem.

Harvey Winkle is the awkward but lovable, goofy and endearing love interest that Sabrina Spellman finds herself fawning over. While the character in the 1996 series was popular thanks to his status as an athlete, times have moved on, and there are many other qualities that earn people a stamp of popularity and approval in high school settings, which opens the door for an updated take on Harvey, played by Jack Martin.

Martin has only recently started to land credited roles on the screen with his career taking off in 2020. Since then, he has tackled a number of small parts in TV and film, before landing a central role in the 2026 comedy series, Not Suitable For Work. It's here that Martin proved himself as the perfect cast for a part like Harvey Kinkle, and with that series getting axed after its debut season, Martin has some space on his schedule.

Libby Chessler is the quintessentially popular girl in a high school setting who is the head of the cheerleading squad, and who is frequently seen belittling and mocking other girls for being weird, or as Libby liberally refers to her classmates such as Sabrina, "freaks."

As an athlete, it makes sense to cast a performer like Maddie Ziegler who can bring the physicality to the role to convincingly play this part, while also having a cold and intense outward face that could inspire fear in others and establish her as a dominant force.

On the back of his performance as Phil Dunphy in Modern Family, Ty Burrell is an outstanding actor who brings so much physicality, personality, and incredible humor to his roles. All of that would go a long way to depicting one of the most fascinating mainstays of the original show, Principal Willard Kraft.

Principal Kraft is a bit of a bumbling buffoon who has an intense crush on Sabrina's aunt Hilda, but throughout the show's run ends up sparking relationships with both aunts at different points. Burrell would be an incredible addition to the lineup, and one that could reasonably juggle all of the different facets of the character that becomes so integral throughout the show's run.

In season 1 of Sabrina the Teenage Witch, Sabrina has a best friend by the name of Jenny Kelley. Jenny is quirky, studious, and eccentric, which means she matches Sabrina's energy as a young witch without knowing her friend's newfound secret. Together, the pair get up to all sorts of misadventures, and they appear to have a powerful bond that brings them together.

However, the series mysteriously replaced the character from season 2 onward. Regardless, a reboot could bring Jenny Kelley back into the story with an actor like Anna Cathcart who delivered an outstanding performance in To All the Boys and XO, Kitty, making her a more central and enduring part of the narrative. Cathcart perfectly embodies the traits of this character, and would make a great contrast to the energy that Ruby Stokes could bring to Sabrina.

In seasons 2 and 3, Valerie Birkhead takes Jenny's place as Sabrina's best friend, and the show avoids referencing Jenny ever again. Valerie then asserts herself as the new best friend, though the energy she brings to the part is much more self-conscious, shy, and awkward. And in a reboot, it would be great to have Sabrina matched up with both Jenny and Valerie to flesh out the cast and give the character more to bounce off.

So, to tackle this role, an experienced comedy actor with a talent for playing more reserved and quiet roles makes the most sense, which is why Iris Apatow is a perfect fit. Apatow has perfected the portrayal of teenage neuroticism that is central to playing the role of Valerie, and her presence opposite Ruby Stokes would be a pleasure to watch unfold.

All in all, while an updated take on Sabrina the Teenage Witch would no doubt look very different to what came before, the talent to pull off such a reboot is perfectly positioned to make an updated version of the series that captures the magic of the original.
